4.2.
The
a_{i,\tau(i)}=0
case

Suppose that
a_{i,\tau(i)}=0
. In this case, we have
\mathbf{U}\simeq U_{q}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})\otimes U_{q}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})
, and there exists an algebra isomorphism
U_{q}(\mathfrak{sl}_{2})\rightarrow\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
which sends
E,F,K^{\pm 1}
to
B_{\tau(i)},B_{i},k_{i}^{\pm 1}
. Hence, for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, there exists an
(n+1)
-dimensional irreducible
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module
V^{\imath}(n)=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{n}\mathbb{K}v_{k}
such that

B_{\tau(i)}v_{0}=0,\quad B_{i}^{(k)}v_{0}=v_{k},\quad k_{i}v_{0}=q_{i}^{n}v_{0}.

Here, we understand that
v_{-1}=v_{n+1}=0
. This
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module has an
X^{\imath}
-weight module structure such that

V^{\imath}(n)=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{n}V^{\imath}(n)_{\zeta_{k}},\quad V^{\imath}(n)_{\zeta_{k}}=\mathbb{K}v_{k},

where
\zeta_{k}\in X^{\imath}
is such that
\langle h_{i}-h_{\tau(i)},\zeta_{k}\rangle=n-2k
.

Set

\mathcal{L}^{\imath}(n):=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{n}\mathbb{K}_{\infty}v_{k},\quad b_{k}:=\mathrm{ev}_{\infty}(v_{k}),

and

\mathcal{B}^{\imath}(n):=\{b_{k}\mid 0\leq k\leq n\}.

Let
M
be an
X^{\imath}
-weight module isomorphic to a direct sum of
V^{\imath}(n)
’s. For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, let
M[n]
denote the isotypic component of
M
of type
V^{\imath}(n)
. For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
0\leq k\leq n
, set

M[n;k]:=B_{i}^{k}(M[n]\cap\operatorname{Ker}B_{\tau(i)}).

Then, we have

M=\bigoplus_{0\leq k\leq n}M[n;k].

For each
v\in M[n,k]
, we set

\beta_{i}(v):=n-k,\quad\beta_{\tau(i)}(v):=k,\quad\widetilde{B}_{i}v:=\frac{1}{[k+1]_{i}}B_{i}v,\quad\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}v:=\frac{1}{[n-k+1]_{i}}B_{\tau(i)}v.

Note that
M[n;k]\subset M_{\zeta_{k}}
, where
\zeta_{k}\in X^{\imath}
is as before, and
\widetilde{B}_{i},\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}
define a
\mathbb{K}
-linear endomorphism on
M
.

Then, one can equip an
\imath
crystal structure on
\mathcal{B}^{\imath}(n)
.
Note that this
\imath
crystal is the same as the one in Example
3.3.10
(
5
).

Remark 4.2.1
.

If we interchange
i
and
\tau(i)
, then
V^{\imath}(n)
becomes
V^{\imath}(n)
with
v_{k}
being replaced by
v_{n-k}
. Hence, our definition of
\beta_{j},\widetilde{B}_{j}
for
j\in\{i,\tau(i)\}
is independent of the choice of
I_{\tau}
.

Lemma 4.2.2
.

For each
n\geq 0
, the pair
(\mathcal{L}^{\imath}(n),\mathcal{B}^{\imath}(n))
is an
\imath
crystal base of
V^{\imath}(n)
. In particular, the crystal base
(\mathcal{L}(0),\mathcal{B}(0))
of the trivial
\mathbf{U}
-module
V(0)
is an
\imath
crystal base such that the
\imath
crystal structure of
\mathcal{B}(0)
is isomorphic to the
\imath
crystal
\mathcal{B}^{\imath}(0)
.

Proof.

The first assertion is straightforwardly verified. The second assertion follows from the easily verified fact that
V(0)\simeq V^{\imath}(0)
as
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-modules.
∎

Proposition 4.2.3
.

Let
M
be a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module isomorphic to a direct sum of various
V^{\imath}(n)
with an
\imath
crystal base
(\mathcal{L}_{M},\mathcal{B}_{M})
, and
N
an integrable
\mathbf{U}
-module with a crystal base
(\mathcal{L}_{N},\mathcal{B}_{N})
. Then, the tensor product
M\otimes N
is isomorphic to a direct sum of various
V^{\imath}(n)
. Moreover, for each
b_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{M}
,
b_{2}\in\mathcal{B}_{N}
and
j\in\{i,\tau(i)\}
, the value
\beta_{j}(b_{1}\otimes b_{2})
and the vector
\widetilde{B}_{j}(b_{1}\otimes b_{2})
are well-defined; we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\beta_{j}(b_{1}\otimes b_{2})&=\max(\varphi_{j}(b_{2})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{j}(b_{1})-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(j)}(b_{2}),\beta_{j}(b_{1})-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(j)}(b_{2}),\varepsilon_{\tau(j)}(b_{2})),\\
\widetilde{B}_{j}(b_{1}\otimes b_{2})&=\begin{cases}b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{j}b_{2}\quad&\text{ if }\varphi_{j}(b_{2})>\beta_{\tau(j)}(b_{1}),\varphi_{\tau(j)}(b_{2})-\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{j}(b_{1}),\\
\widetilde{B}_{j}b_{1}\otimes b_{2}\quad&\text{ if }\varphi_{j}(b_{2})\leq\beta_{\tau(j)}(b_{1})>\varphi_{\tau(j)}(b_{2})-\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{j}(b_{1}),\\
b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(j)}b_{2}\quad&\text{ if }\varphi_{j}(b_{2}),\beta_{\tau(j)}(b_{1})\leq\varphi_{\tau(j)}(b_{2})-\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{j}(b_{1}).\end{cases}\end{split}

Proof.

The assertion can be proved essentially in the same way as
[
16
, Theorem 6.3.5]
.
∎

Corollary 4.2.4
.

Let
M
be an integrable
\mathbf{U}
-module with a crystal base
(\mathcal{L}_{M},\mathcal{B}_{M})
. Then, for each
b\in\mathcal{B}_{M}
and
j\in\{i,\tau(i)\}
, the value
\beta_{j}(b_{1}\otimes b_{2})
and the vector
\widetilde{B}_{j}(b_{1}\otimes b_{2})
are well-defined; we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{j}(b)=\max(\varphi_{j}(b)-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(j)}(b),\varepsilon_{\tau(j)}(b)),\\
&\widetilde{B}_{j}b=\begin{cases}\widetilde{F}_{j}b\quad&\text{ if }\varphi_{j}(b)>\varphi_{\tau(j)}(b),\\
\widetilde{E}_{\tau(j)}b\quad&\text{ if }\varphi_{j}(b)\leq\varphi_{\tau(j)}(b).\end{cases}\end{split}

Proof.

The assertion follows from Lemma
4.2.2
and Proposition
4.2.3
by identifying
\mathcal{B}_{M}
with
\mathcal{B}^{\imath}(0)\otimes\mathcal{B}_{M}
.
∎

Example 4.2.5
.

Let
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, and consider the
(m+1)(n+1)
-dimensional irreducible
\mathbf{U}
-module
V(m,n)=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k\leq m\\
0\leq l\leq n\end{subarray}}\mathbb{K}v_{k,l}
given by

E_{i}v_{0,0}=E_{\tau(i)}v_{0,0}=0,\quad F_{\tau(i)}^{(k)}F_{i}^{(l)}v_{0,0}=v_{k,l},\quad K_{i}v_{0,0}=q_{i}^{n}v_{0,0},\quad K_{\tau(i)}v_{0,0}=q_{i}^{m}v_{0,0}.

It has a crystal basis
\mathcal{B}(m,n):=\{b_{k,l}\mid 0\leq k\leq m,\ 0\leq l\leq n\}
given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\operatorname{wt}_{i}(b_{k,l})=n-2l,\ \varepsilon_{i}(b_{k,l})=l,\ \varphi_{i}(b_{k,l})=n-l,\ \widetilde{E}_{i}b_{k,l}=b_{k,l-1},\ \widetilde{F}_{i}b_{k,l}=b_{k,l+1},\\
&\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}(b_{k,l})=m-2k,\ \varepsilon_{\tau(i)}(b_{k,l})=k,\ \varphi_{\tau(i)}(b_{k,l})=m-k,\ \widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{k,l}=b_{k-1,l},\ \widetilde{F}_{\tau(i)}b_{k,l}=b_{k+1,l}.\end{split}

Hence, we have

\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{k,l}=\begin{cases}b_{k,l+1}&\text{ if }n-l>m-k,\\
b_{k-1,l}&\text{ if }n-l\leq m-k.\end{cases}

For example, when
(m,n)=(2,3)
, the
\widetilde{B}_{i}
on
\mathcal{B}(m,n)
is described as follows (the label “
i
” is omitted since we have
I_{\tau}=\{i\}
):

\textstyle{b_{2,0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{b_{2,1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{b_{2,2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{b_{2,3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{b_{1,0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{b_{1,1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{b_{1,2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{b_{1,3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{b_{0,0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{b_{0,1}}
\textstyle{b_{0,2}}
\textstyle{b_{0,3}}

From above, we see that the
\widetilde{B}_{i}
(resp.,
\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}
) on a crystal basis of an integrable
\mathbf{U}
-module coincides with the
\widetilde{F}_{\tau(i)^{\prime}}
(resp.,
\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)^{\prime}}
) on the tensor product of crystal bases of two integrable
\mathbf{U}_{I^{\prime}}
-modules (see Examples
2.2.3
,
3.1.1
, and
3.3.4
).

4.3.
The
a_{i,\tau(i)}=-1
case

Suppose that
a_{i,\tau(i)}=-1
. In this case, we have
\mathbf{U}\simeq U_{q}(\mathfrak{sl}_{3})
, and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&k_{i}B_{i}=q_{i}^{-3}B_{i}k_{i},\quad k_{i}B_{\tau(i)}=q_{i}^{3}B_{\tau(i)}k_{i},\\
&B_{i}^{2}B_{\tau(i)}-[2]_{i}B_{i}B_{\tau(i)}B_{i}+B_{\tau(i)}B_{i}^{2}=-[2]_{i}B_{i}\{k_{i};-1-s_{i}\}_{i},\\
&B_{\tau(i)}^{2}B_{i}-[2]_{i}B_{\tau(i)}B_{i}B_{\tau(i)}+B_{i}B_{\tau(i)}^{2}=-[2]_{i}\{k_{i};-1-s_{i}\}_{i}B_{\tau(i)},\end{split}

where

\{k_{i};a\}_{i}:=q_{i}^{a}k_{i}+q_{i}^{-a}k_{i}^{-1}

for each
a\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Set

t:=B_{\tau(i)}B_{i}-q_{i}B_{i}B_{\tau(i)}-[k_{i};-s_{i}]_{i},

where

[k_{i};a]_{i}:=\frac{q_{i}^{a}k_{i}-q_{i}^{-a}k_{i}^{-1}}{q_{i}-q_{i}^{-1}}.

Then, for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we have

(7)

\displaystyle\begin{split}B_{\tau(i)}B_{i}^{(k)}=B_{i}^{(k-1)}(t+[k_{i};-s_{i}-2(k-1)]_{i})+q_{i}^{k}B_{i}^{(k)}B_{\tau(i)}.\end{split}

For each
n_{-}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
n_{+}\in\mathbb{Z}
, let
V^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+})=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{n_{-}}\mathbb{K}v_{n}
be a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module given by

B_{\tau(i)}v_{0}=0,\quad B_{i}^{(k)}v_{0}=v_{k},\quad k_{i}v_{0}=q_{i}^{n_{-}+n_{+}}v_{0},\quad tv_{0}=[n_{-}-n_{+}+s_{i}]_{i}v_{0},

where,
v_{-1}=v_{n_{-}+1}=0
. Then,
V^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+})
is an irreducible (see
[
17
, Theorem 4.4.7]
)
X^{\imath}
-weight module such that

V^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+})=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{n_{-}}V^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+})_{\zeta_{k}},\quad V^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+})_{\zeta_{k}}=\mathbb{K}v_{k},

where
\zeta_{k}\in X^{\imath}
is such that
\langle h_{i}-h_{\tau(i)},\zeta_{k}\rangle=n_{-}+n_{+}-3k
. Furthermore,
V^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+})
admits a contragredient Hermitian inner product
(\cdot,\cdot)
such that
(v_{0},v_{0})=1
.

Remark 4.3.1
.

Note that we have

B_{i}v_{n_{-}}=0,\quad B_{\tau(i)}^{(k)}v_{n_{-}}\in\mathbb{K}^{\times}v_{n_{-}-k},\quad k_{\tau(i)}v_{n_{-}}=q_{i}^{2n_{-}-n_{+}},\quad t^{\prime}v_{n_{-}}=[n_{+}+s_{\tau(i)}]v_{n_{-}},

where

t^{\prime}:=B_{i}B_{\tau(i)}-q_{i}B_{\tau(i)}B_{i}-[k_{\tau(i)};-s_{\tau(i)}]_{i}.

This shows that if we exchange the roles of
i
and
\tau(i)
, then
n_{+},k
are replaced by
n_{-}-n_{+},n_{-}-k
, respectively.

Lemma 4.3.2
.

For each
0\leq k\leq n_{-}
, we have

(v_{k},v_{k})=q_{i}^{k(-n_{-}-n_{+}+\frac{3}{2}k+s_{i}-\frac{1}{2})}{n_{-}\brack k}_{i}\prod_{l=1}^{k}\{n_{+}-s_{i}-l+1\}_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}[q,q^{-1}],

where

\{a\}_{i}:=q_{i}^{a}+q_{i}^{-a}

for each
a\in\mathbb{Z}
. Consequently, if we write
(v_{k},v_{k})=\operatorname{lt}(v_{k})^{2}+\text{lower terms}
for some
\operatorname{lt}(v_{k})\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}q^{\mathbb{Z}}
, then we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\operatorname{lt}(v_{k})=\begin{cases}1&\text{ if }n_{+}-s_{i}\geq n_{-}\text{ or },\\
&\ -1<n_{+}-s_{i}<n_{-}\and k<n_{+}-s_{i}+1,\\
\sqrt{2}q_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}(k-n_{+}+s_{i}-1)(k-n_{+}+s_{i})}&\text{ if }-1<n_{+}-s_{i}<n_{-}\and k\geq n_{+}-s_{i}+1,\\
q_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}k(k-2n_{+}+2s_{i}-1)}&\text{ if }n_{+}-s_{i}\leq-1.\end{cases}\end{split}

Proof.

Let us see that

\wp^{*}(B_{i})=q_{i}^{-1}E_{i}K_{i}^{-1}+q_{i}^{s_{i}}K_{i}^{-1}q_{i}^{-1}F_{\tau(i)}K_{\tau(i)}=q_{i}^{s_{i}-2}B_{\tau(i)}k_{i}^{-1}.

Also, by identity (
7
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}B_{\tau(i)}v_{k}&=B_{\tau(i)}B_{i}^{(k)}v_{0}\\
&=([n_{-}-n_{+}+s_{i}]_{i}+[n_{-}+n_{+}-s_{i}-2k+2]_{i})v_{k-1}\\
&=[n_{-}-k+1]_{i}\{n_{+}-s_{i}-k+1\}_{i}v_{k-1}.\end{split}

Now, we compute as

\displaystyle\begin{split}(v_{k},v_{k})&=\frac{1}{[k]_{i}}(v_{k-1},q_{i}^{s_{i}-2}B_{\tau(i)}k_{i}^{-1}v_{k})\\
&=\frac{q_{i}^{-n_{-}-n_{+}+3k+s_{i}-2}[n_{-}-k+1]_{i}\{n_{+}-s_{i}-k+1\}_{i}}{[k]_{i}}(v_{k-1},v_{k-1})\\
&=\prod_{l=1}^{k}\frac{q_{i}^{-n_{-}-n_{+}+3l+s_{i}-2}[n_{-}-l+1]_{i}\{n_{+}-s_{i}-l+1\}_{i}}{[l]_{i}}(v_{0},v_{0})\\
&=q_{i}^{k(-n_{-}-n_{+}+\frac{3}{2}k+s_{i}-\frac{1}{2})}{n_{-}\brack k}_{i}\prod_{l=1}^{k}\{n_{+}-s_{i}-l+1\}_{i}.\end{split}

This proves the first half of the assertion. The remaining assertion follows from this identity by noting that the leading term of
\{a\}_{i}
is
2^{\delta_{a,0}}q_{i}^{|a|}
.
∎

For each
k
, set

\widetilde{v}_{k}:=\operatorname{lt}(v_{k})^{-1}v_{k}.

Such
\widetilde{v}_{k}
is characterized up to a multiple of
1+q^{-1}\mathbb{K}_{\infty}
by the conditions that
\widetilde{v}_{k}\in\mathbb{K}^{\times}v_{k}
and
(\widetilde{v}_{k},\widetilde{v}_{k})\in 1+q^{-1}\mathbb{K}_{\infty}
. Now, we define linear operators
\widetilde{B}_{i},\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}
by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widetilde{B}_{i}\widetilde{v}_{k}=\begin{cases}\widetilde{v}_{k+1}&\text{ if }0\leq k<n_{-},\\
0&\text{ if }k=n_{-},\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}\widetilde{v}_{k}=\begin{cases}\widetilde{v}_{k}&\text{ if }0<k\leq n_{-},\\
0&\text{ if }k=0.\end{cases}\end{split}

Note that
\widetilde{B}_{i},\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}
are independent of the choice of
I_{\tau}
up to
1+q^{-1}\mathbb{K}_{\infty}
(see Remark
4.3.1
). In particular, they are actually independent at
q=\infty
.

Set

\mathcal{L}^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+}):=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{n_{-}}\mathbb{K}_{\infty}\widetilde{v}_{k},\quad b_{k}:=\mathrm{ev}_{\infty}(\widetilde{v}_{k})

and

\mathcal{B}^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+}):=\{b_{k}\mid 0\leq k\leq n_{-}\}.

Let
M
be an
X^{\imath}
-weight module isomorphic to a direct sum of
V^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+})
’s. The linear operators
\widetilde{B}_{i},\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}
on
V^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+})
’s can be extended to
M
. For each
n_{-},n_{+}
, let
M[n_{-},n_{+}]
denote the isotypic component of
M
of type
V^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+})
. For each
0\leq k\leq n_{-}
, set

M[n_{-},n_{+};k]:=B_{i}^{k}(M[n_{-},n_{+}]\cap\operatorname{Ker}B_{\tau(i)}).

Then, we have

M=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k\leq n_{-}\\
n_{+}\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}M[n_{-},n_{+};k].

For each
v\in M[n_{-},n_{+};k]
, we set

\beta_{i}(v)=n_{-}-k+\max(n_{+}-s_{i}-k,0),\quad\beta_{\tau(i)}(v)=k+\max(-n_{+}-s_{\tau(i)}+k,0).

Again, note that
\beta_{i},\beta_{\tau(i)}
are independent of the choice of
I_{\tau}
(see Remark
4.3.1
).

Then, one can equip an
\imath
crystal structure on
\mathcal{B}^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+})
.
Note that this
\imath
crystal is the same as the one in Example
3.3.10
(
6
).

Lemma 4.3.3
.

For each
n_{-}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
n_{+}\in\mathbb{Z}
, the pair
(\mathcal{L}^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+}),\mathcal{B}^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+}))
is an
\imath
crystal base of
V^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+})
. In particular, the crystal base
(\mathcal{L}(0),\mathcal{B}(0))
of the trivial
\mathbf{U}
-module
V(0)
is an
\imath
crystal base such that the
\imath
crystal structure of
\mathcal{B}(0)
is isomorphic to the
\imath
crystal
\mathcal{B}^{\imath}(0,0)
.

Proof.

The first assertion is straightforwardly verified. The second assertion follows from the easily verified fact that
V(0)\simeq V^{\imath}(0,0)
as
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-modules.
∎

Below, we aim to describe the tensor product rule for
\widetilde{B}_{i}
. Recall that
\mathbf{U}\simeq U_{q}(\mathfrak{sl}_{3})
. Let us take this isomorphism in a way such that the natural representation
V_{\natural}=\mathbb{K}u_{-1}\oplus\mathbb{K}u_{0}\oplus\mathbb{K}u_{1}
of
U_{q}(\mathfrak{sl}_{3})
has the following
\mathbf{U}
-module structure:

E_{i}u_{-1}=E_{\tau(i)}u_{-1}=0,\ K_{i}u_{-1}=q_{i}u_{-1},\ K_{\tau(i)}u_{-1}=u_{-1},\ F_{i}u_{-1}=u_{0},\ F_{\tau(i)}u_{0}=u_{1}.

Let
(\cdot,\cdot)
denote the contragredient Hermitian inner product on the
\mathbf{U}
-module
V_{\natural}
such that
(u_{-1},u_{-1})=1
. Also, set
\mathcal{L}_{\natural}:=\mathcal{L}_{V_{\natural}}
,
\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{\natural}:=\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{V_{\natural}}
,
\mathcal{B}_{\natural}:=\{b_{j}:=\mathrm{ev}_{\infty}(u_{j})\mid j=-1,0,1\}
. Then,
(\mathcal{L}_{\natural},\mathcal{B}_{\natural})
is the crystal base of
V_{\natural}
.

Set
V:=V^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+})\otimes V_{\natural}
,
\mathcal{L}:=\mathcal{L}_{V^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+})}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\natural}
, and

\mathcal{B}:=\mathcal{B}^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+})\otimes\mathcal{B}_{\natural}=\{b\otimes b^{\prime}\mid(b,b^{\prime})\in\mathcal{B}^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+})\times\mathcal{B}_{\natural}\}.

The following propositions describe
\widetilde{B}_{i}
on
\mathcal{L}
modulo
q^{-1}\mathcal{L}
. The proofs are based on straightforward calculation with no technical argument needed, hence we omit them.

Proposition 4.3.4
.

If
n_{-}=0
, then we have

V\simeq V^{\imath}(1,n_{+})\oplus V^{\imath}(0,n_{+}+1),

with highest weight vectors

\displaystyle\begin{split}&v_{+0}:=v_{0}\otimes u_{-1}+q_{i}^{-(n_{+}-s_{i})}v_{0}\otimes u_{1},\\
&v_{0+}:=v_{0}\otimes u_{-1}-q_{i}^{+(n_{+}-s_{i})}v_{0}\otimes u_{1}.\end{split}

Furthermore, we have

B_{i}v_{+0}=q_{i}^{-(n_{+}-s_{i})}\{n_{+}-s_{i}\}_{i}v_{0}\otimes u_{0}

By normalizing the three vectors above, we see that
(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B})
forms an
\imath
crystal base of

such that the crystal graph of

is described as follows (the label “

” is omitted since we have

. The same applies to the following propositions).

When

, we have

:

\textstyle{b_{-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

:

Proposition 4.3.5
.

If
n_{-}>0
, then, we have

V\simeq V^{\imath}(n_{-}+1,n_{+})\oplus V^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+}+1)\oplus V^{\imath}(n_{-}-1,n_{+}-1),

with highest weight vectors

\displaystyle\begin{split}&v_{+0}:=v_{0}\otimes u_{-1}+q_{i}^{n_{-}-(n_{+}-s_{i})}v_{0}\otimes u_{1},\\
&v_{0+}:=v_{0}\otimes u_{-1}-q_{i}^{-n_{-}+(n_{+}-s_{i})}v_{0}\otimes u_{1},\\
&v_{--}:=v_{1}\otimes u_{-1}+q_{i}^{-n_{-}-(n_{+}-s_{i})-1}v_{1}\otimes u_{1}-q_{i}^{-n_{-}-(n_{+}-s_{i})}[n_{-}]_{i}\{n_{+}-s_{i}\}_{i}v_{0}\otimes u_{0}.\end{split}

Furthermore, for each
k\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}B_{i}^{(k)}v_{+0}&=q_{i}^{-k}v_{k}\otimes u_{-1}+q_{i}^{n_{-}-(n_{+}-s_{i})}v_{k}\otimes u_{1}+q_{i}^{-(n_{+}-s_{i})+k-1}\{(n_{+}-s_{i})-k+1\}_{i}v_{k-1}\otimes u_{0},\\

&\quad-q_{i}^{-n_{-}-(n_{+}-s_{i})+k}[n_{-}-k]_{i}\{(n_{+}-s_{i})-k\}_{i}v_{k}\otimes u_{0}.\end{split}

Proposition 4.3.6
.

. Set

, we have

is described as follows:

\textstyle{b_{-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Proposition 4.3.7
.

Suppose that
0<n_{-}=n_{+}-s_{i}
. Set

\widetilde{v}_{+0}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}v_{+0},\quad\widetilde{v}_{0+}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}v_{0+},\quad\widetilde{v}_{--}:=v_{--}.

Then, modulo
q^{-1}\mathcal{L}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widetilde{B}_{i}^{k}\widetilde{v}_{+0}\equiv\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\widetilde{v}_{0}\otimes u_{-1}+\widetilde{v}_{0}\otimes u_{1})\quad&\text{ if }k=0,\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\widetilde{v}_{k-1}\otimes u_{0}+\widetilde{v}_{k}\otimes u_{1})\quad&\text{ if }1\leq k\leq n_{-},\\
\widetilde{v}_{n_{-}}\otimes u_{0}\quad&\text{ if }k=n_{-}+1,\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}^{k}\widetilde{v}_{0+}\equiv\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\widetilde{v}_{0}\otimes u_{-1}-\widetilde{v}_{0}\otimes u_{1})\quad&\text{ if }k=0,\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\widetilde{v}_{k-1}\otimes u_{0}-\widetilde{v}_{k}\otimes u_{1})\quad&\text{ if }1\leq k\leq n_{-},\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}^{k}\widetilde{v}_{--}\equiv\widetilde{v}_{k+1}\otimes u_{-1}\quad\text{ if }0\leq k\leq n_{-}-1,\end{split}

Consequently,
(\mathcal{L},\mathcal{B})
forms an
\imath
crystal basis of
V
such that
\mathcal{B}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{B}^{\imath}(n_{-}+1,n_{+};\vee)\sqcup\mathcal{B}^{\imath}(n_{-}-1,n_{+}-1)
. The crystal graph of
\mathcal{B}
is described as follows:

\textstyle{b_{-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Proposition 4.3.10
.

n_{-}>0

. The crystal graph of
\mathcal{B}
is described as follows:

\textstyle{b_{-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{b_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau(i)}
\textstyle{b_{1}}

\textstyle{\bullet\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\vdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Since
\beta_{i}(b),\beta_{\tau(i)}(b)
for
b\in\mathcal{B}^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+})
,
\mathcal{B}^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+};\vee)
,
\mathcal{B}^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+};\wedge)
can be read from the crystal graphs, Propositions
4.3.6
–
4.3.10
can be reformulated as follows:

Proposition 4.3.11
.

Let
M
be a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module isomorphic to a direct sum of various
V^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+})
with an
\imath
crystal base
(\mathcal{L}_{M},\mathcal{B}_{M})
. Then,
M\otimes V_{\natural}
is isomorphic to a direct sum of various
V^{\imath}(n_{-},n_{+})
. Moreover, for each
b\in\mathcal{B}_{M}
,
j\in\{i,\tau(i)\}
, and
k\in\{-1,0,1\}
, the value
\beta_{j}(b\otimes b_{k})
and the vector
\widetilde{B}_{j}(b\otimes b_{k})
are well-defined; if we set
\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{j}:=\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{j}(b)
and
\beta_{j}:=\beta_{j}(b)
, then we have the following:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b\otimes b_{-1})=\begin{cases}\beta_{i}+1&\text{ if }\beta_{\tau(i)}=0\and\beta_{i}=\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i},\\
\beta_{i}&\text{ otherwise},\end{cases}\\
&\beta_{\tau(i)}(b\otimes b_{-1})=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }\beta_{\tau(i)}=0,\\
\beta_{\tau(i)}-1&\text{ if }\beta_{\tau(i)}>0,\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}(b\otimes b_{-1})=\begin{cases}b\otimes b_{0}&\text{ if }\beta_{\tau(i)}=0<\beta_{i}=\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i},\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}b\otimes b_{0}&\text{ if }\beta_{\tau(i)}=0=\beta_{i}=\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i},\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\widetilde{B}_{i}b\otimes b_{-1}+b\otimes b_{0})&\text{ if }\beta_{\tau(i)}=0<\beta_{i}\neq\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i},\\
\widetilde{B}_{i}b\otimes b_{-1}&\text{ otherwise},\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}(b\otimes b_{-1})=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }\beta_{\tau(i)}\leq 1,\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b\otimes b_{-1}&\text{ if }\beta_{\tau(i)}=2\and\beta_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b)=0,\\
\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b\otimes b_{-1}&\text{ otherwise},\end{cases}\end{split}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b\otimes b_{0})=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }\beta_{i}=0,\\
\beta_{i}-1&\text{ if }\beta_{i}>0,\end{cases}\\
&\beta_{\tau(i)}(b\otimes b_{0})=\begin{cases}\beta_{\tau(i)}+2&\text{ if }\beta_{i}=0\and\beta_{i}\neq\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i},\\
\beta_{\tau(i)}+1&\text{ otherwise},\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}(b\otimes b_{0})=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }\beta_{i}\leq 1,\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\widetilde{B}_{i}b\otimes b_{0}&\text{ if }\beta_{i}=2\and\beta_{i}(\widetilde{B}_{i}b)=0,\\
\widetilde{B}_{i}b\otimes b_{0}&\text{ otherwise},\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}(b\otimes b_{0})=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(b\otimes b_{-1}+b\otimes b_{1})&\text{ if }\beta_{i}=0=\beta_{\tau(i)}\and\beta_{i}=\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i},\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b\otimes b_{0}+b\otimes b_{1})&\text{ if }\beta_{i}=0<\beta_{\tau(i)}\and\beta_{i}=\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i},\\
b\otimes b_{1}&\text{ if }\beta_{i}=0\and\beta_{i}\neq\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i},\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}b\otimes b_{-1}&\text{ if }\beta_{\tau(i)}=0<\beta_{i}\neq\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i},\\
b\otimes b_{-1}&\text{ if }\beta_{\tau(i)}=0<\beta_{i}=\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i},\\
\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b\otimes b_{0}&\text{ otherwise},\end{cases}\end{split}

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b\otimes b_{1})=\beta_{i}+1,\\
&\beta_{\tau(i)}(b\otimes b_{1})=\beta_{\tau(i)},\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}(b\otimes b_{1})=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}b\otimes b_{0}&\text{ if }\beta_{i}=0\and\beta_{i}=\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i},\\
b\otimes b_{0}&\text{ if }\beta_{i}=0\and\beta_{i}\neq\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i},\\
\widetilde{B}_{i}b\otimes b_{1}&\text{ otherwise},\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}(b\otimes b_{1})=\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b\otimes b_{1}.\end{split}

Corollary 4.3.12
.

Let
M
be an integrable
\mathbf{U}
-module with a crystal base
(\mathcal{L}_{M},\mathcal{B}_{M})
. Then, the values
\beta_{i}(b),\beta_{\tau(i)}(b)
are defined for all
b\in\mathcal{B}_{M}
. Also,
\widetilde{B}_{i},\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}
preserve
\mathcal{L}_{M}
.

Proof.

Since
M
can be embedded into a direct sum of
V_{\natural}^{\otimes N}
for various
N\geq 0
, the assertion follows inductively from Lemma
4.3.3
and Proposition
4.3.11
by identifying
\mathcal{B}(0)\otimes\mathcal{B}_{M}
with
\mathcal{B}_{M}
.
∎

Remark 4.3.13
.

Let
M
be as before. The
\beta_{j}(b),\widetilde{B}_{j}b
for
j\in\{i,\tau(i)\}
,
b\in\mathcal{B}_{M}
can be inductively calculated by Proposition
4.3.11
. Explicit formulas for them will be given later.

5.
Tensor product rule for
\imath
crystal

In this section, we shall construct an
\imath
crystal from an
\imath
crystal
\mathcal{B}_{1}
and a crystal
\mathcal{B}_{2}
satisfying certain conditions, whose underlying set is
\mathcal{B}_{1}\times\mathcal{B}_{2}
. The construction is motivated by the representation theoretic results in the previous section.

5.1.
Statements

Given an
\imath
crystal
\mathcal{B}_{1}
and a crystal
\mathcal{B}_{2}
, consider the direct product
\mathcal{B}:=\mathcal{B}_{1}\times\mathcal{B}_{2}
, and identify
\overline{\mathcal{L}}:=\mathbb{C}\mathcal{B}
with
\overline{\mathcal{L}_{1}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{\mathcal{L}_{2}}
, where
\overline{\mathcal{L}_{i}}:=\mathbb{C}\mathcal{B}_{i}
with
i=1,2
. Then,
\{b_{1}\otimes b_{2}\mid b_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{1},\ b_{2}\in\mathcal{B}_{2}\}
forms an orthonormal basis of
\overline{\mathcal{L}}
with respect to the Hermitian inner product induced by those on
\overline{\mathcal{L}_{1}}
and
\overline{\mathcal{L}_{2}}
making
\mathcal{B}_{1}
and
\mathcal{B}_{2}
orthonormal bases;

(b_{1}\otimes b_{2},b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b^{\prime}_{2}):=(b_{1},b^{\prime}_{1})(b_{2},b^{\prime}_{2}).

We identify
(b_{1},b_{2})\in\mathcal{B}
with
b_{1}\otimes b_{2}
, and write
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{2}
.

For
i\in I
and
b=b_{1}\otimes b_{2}\in\mathcal{B}
, set

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F_{i}(b):=\begin{cases}\varphi_{i}(b_{2})+\delta_{\overline{\beta_{i}(b_{1})+1},\overline{\varphi_{i}(b_{2})}}&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}=2,\\
\varphi_{i}(b_{2})&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}\neq 2,\end{cases}\\
&B_{i}(b):=\begin{cases}\beta_{i}(b_{1})&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}=2,\\
\beta_{i}(b_{1})-\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b_{1})+s_{i}&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}\neq 2,\end{cases}\\
&E_{i}(b):=\begin{cases}\varphi_{i}(b_{2})&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}=2,\\
\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b_{2})-\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b_{1})+s_{i}&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}\neq 2,\end{cases}\\
\end{split}

where we set
s_{i}=0
for all
i\in I
with
a_{i,\tau(i)}=0
. Note that we have
B_{i}(b)=\beta_{\tau(i)}(b_{1})
if
a_{i,\tau(i)}=0
, and

B_{i}(b)=\begin{cases}\beta_{\tau(i)}(b_{1})&\text{ if }\beta_{i}(b_{1})=\beta_{\tau(i)}(b_{1})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b_{1})-s_{i},\\
\beta_{\tau(i)}(b_{1})+1&\text{ if }\beta_{i}(b_{1})\neq\beta_{\tau(i)}(b_{1})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b_{1})-s_{i}\end{cases}

if
a_{i,\tau(i)}=-1
. Also, note that when
a_{i,\tau(i)}=2
, we have

F_{i}(b)=\begin{cases}E_{i}(b)&\text{ if }\overline{\beta_{i}(b_{1})}=\overline{\varphi_{i}(b_{2})},\\
E_{i}(b)+1&\text{ if }\overline{\beta_{i}(b_{1})}\neq\overline{\varphi_{i}(b_{2})}.\end{cases}

Proposition 5.1.1
.

Let
\mathcal{B}_{1}
be an
\imath
crystal,
\mathcal{B}_{2}
a crystal. Assume that
\mathcal{B}_{2}
satisfies conditions (S1)–(S3)’ in Subsection
2.2
for all
i,\tau(i)\in I
with
a_{i,\tau(i)}\neq 2
. For each
b_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{1}
,
b_{2}\in\mathcal{B}_{2}
, and
i\in I
, set
b:=b_{1}\otimes b_{2}
,
\beta_{i}:=\beta_{i}(b_{1})
,
\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}:=\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}(b_{1})
,
\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}:=\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b_{1})
,
\varepsilon_{i}:=\varepsilon_{i}(b_{2})
,
\varphi_{i}:=\varphi_{i}(b_{2})
,
\operatorname{wt}:=\operatorname{wt}(b_{2})
,
\operatorname{wt}_{i}:=\operatorname{wt}_{i}(b_{2})
. Then,
\mathcal{B}:=\mathcal{B}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{2}
is equipped with an
\imath
crystal structure as follows: Let
b_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{1}
,
b_{2}\in\mathcal{B}_{2}
, and
i\in I
.

(1)

\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}(b)=\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}+\overline{\operatorname{wt}}
.

(2)

If
a_{i,\tau(i)}=2
, then

\displaystyle\begin{split}\beta_{i}(b)&=\max(F_{i}(b),B_{i}(b),E_{i}(b))-\operatorname{wt}_{i}\\
&=\begin{cases}\varepsilon_{i}+1&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)>B_{i}(b),E_{i}(b),\\
\beta_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{i}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)\leq B_{i}(b)>E_{i}(b),\\
\varepsilon_{i}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b),B_{i}(b)\leq E_{i}(b),\end{cases}\\
\widetilde{B}_{i}b&=\begin{cases}b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)>B_{i}(b),E_{i}(b),\\
\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\otimes b_{2}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)\leq B_{i}(b)>E_{i}(b),\\
b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{i}b_{2}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b),B_{i}(b)\leq E_{i}(b).\\
\end{cases}\end{split}

Here, we understand that
-\infty<-\infty_{\mathrm{ev}},-\infty_{\mathrm{odd}}<a
for all
a\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(3)

If
a_{i,\tau(i)}=0
, then

\displaystyle\begin{split}\beta_{i}(b)&=\max(F_{i}(b),B_{i}(b),E_{i}(b))+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}\\
&=\begin{cases}\varphi_{i}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)>B_{i}(b),E_{i}(b),\\
\beta_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)\leq B_{i}(b)>E_{i}(b),\\
\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b),B_{i}(b)\leq E_{i}(b),\end{cases}\\
\widetilde{B}_{i}b&=\begin{cases}b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)>B_{i}(b),E_{i}(b),\\
\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\otimes b_{2}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)\leq B_{i}(b)>E_{i}(b),\\
b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b),B_{i}(b)\leq E_{i}(b).\end{cases}\end{split}

(4)

If
a_{i,\tau(i)}=-1
, then we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\beta_{i}(b)&=\max(F_{i}(b),B_{i}(b),E_{i}(b))+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)},\\
&=\begin{cases}\varphi_{i}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)>B_{i}(b),E_{i}(b),\\
\beta_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)\leq B_{i}(b)>E_{i}(b),\\
\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b),B_{i}(b)\leq E_{i}(b).\end{cases}\end{split}

Also, the following hold:

(a)

When
F_{i}(b)>B_{i}(b),E_{i}(b)
,

\widetilde{B}_{i}b=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)=E_{i}(b)+1\and\varphi_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}+1,\\
b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2}&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

Here and below, whenever talking about
\varphi_{i}(b_{2})
for
b_{2}\in\mathcal{B}_{2}\sqcup\{0\}
, we further impose that
b_{2}\in\mathcal{B}_{2}
.

(b)

When
F_{i}(b)\leq B_{i}(b)>E_{i}(b)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widetilde{B}_{i}b=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\otimes b_{2}&\text{ if }B_{i}(b)=E_{i}(b)+1,\and\beta_{i}(\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1})=\beta_{i}-2,\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\otimes b_{2}+b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2})&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)=B_{i}(b)\neq\beta_{\tau(i)},\\
\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\otimes b_{2}&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}\end{split}

Here and below, whenever talking about
\beta_{i}(b_{1})
for
b_{1}\in\mathcal{L}_{1}
, we further impose that
b_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{1}
.

(c)

When
F_{i}(b),B_{i}(b)\leq E_{i}(b)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widetilde{B}_{i}b=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2}&\text{ if }E_{i}(b)=F_{i}(b)\and\varphi_{i}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2})=\varphi_{i},\\
&\text{ or }E_{i}(b)=B_{i}(b)=\beta_{\tau(i)}\and\varphi_{i}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2})<E_{i}(b),\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2}+b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2})&\text{ if }E_{i}(b)=F_{i}(b)>\beta_{\tau(i)}\and\varphi_{i}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2})=\varphi_{i}-1,\\
b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2}&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}\end{split}

Since the proof of this proposition is lengthy and independent of the later argument, we put it in Subsection
7.1
.

Remark 5.1.2
.

The tensor product rule above for
I=\{i,\tau(i)\}
with
a_{i,\tau(i)}\neq-1
coincides with the one given in Propositions
4.1.2
and
4.2.3
. When
a_{i,\tau(i)}=-1
and
\mathcal{B}_{2}=\mathcal{B}_{\natural}
, it coincides with the one given in Proposition
4.3.11
.

Proposition 5.1.3
.

The tensor product of an
\imath
crystal and a crystal is associative. Namely, let
\mathcal{B}_{1}
be an
\imath
crystal, and
\mathcal{B}_{2},\mathcal{B}_{3}
crystals such that
\mathcal{B}_{2},\mathcal{B}_{3},\mathcal{B}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{3}
satisfy conditions
(S1)
–
(S3)’
for all
i,\tau(i)\in I
with
a_{i,\tau(i)}\neq 2
. Then, the canonical map

\mathcal{B}_{1}\otimes(\mathcal{B}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{3})\rightarrow(\mathcal{B}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{2})\otimes\mathcal{B}_{3};\ b_{1}\otimes(b_{2}\otimes b_{3})\mapsto(b_{1}\otimes b_{2})\otimes b_{3}

gives rise to an isomorphism of
\imath
crystals.

Since the proof of this proposition is lengthy and independent of the later argument, we put it in Subsection
7.2
.

Remark 5.1.4
.

Suppose that
I=\{i,\tau(i)\}
for some
i\in I
. Let
V_{\natural}
denote the natural representation of
\mathbf{U}
, and
\mathcal{B}_{\natural}
its crystal basis. By Remark
5.1.2
and Proposition
5.1.3
, the
\beta_{i}
and
\widetilde{B}_{i}
on
\mathcal{B}_{\natural}^{\otimes N}=\mathcal{B}(0)\otimes\mathcal{B}_{\natural}^{\otimes N}
combinatorially defined in this section coincide with the ones representation theoretically defined in the previous section.

5.2.
Consequences

In this subsection, we list some consequences of Propositions
5.1.1
and
5.1.3
.

Corollary 5.2.1
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a crystal satisfying conditions
(S1)
–
(S3)’
for all
i,\tau(i)\in I
with
a_{i,\tau(i)}\neq 2
. For each
b\in\mathcal{B}
and
i\in I
, set
\varphi_{i}:=\varphi_{i}(b)
,
\varepsilon_{i}:=\varepsilon_{i}(b)
,
\operatorname{wt}:=\operatorname{wt}(b)
, and
\operatorname{wt}_{i}:=\operatorname{wt}_{i}(b)
. Then, it has an
\imath
crystal structure as follows: Let
b\in\mathcal{B}
and
i\in I
.

(1)

\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}(b)=\overline{\operatorname{wt}}
.

(2)

If
a_{i,\tau(i)}=2
, then

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b)=\begin{cases}\varepsilon_{i}+1&\text{ if }|s_{i}|\leq\varphi_{i}\and\overline{s_{i}}\neq\overline{\varphi_{i}},\\
|s_{i}|-\operatorname{wt}_{i}&\text{ if }|s_{i}|>\varphi_{i},\\
\varepsilon_{i}&\text{ if }|s_{i}|\leq\varphi_{i}\and\overline{s_{i}}=\overline{\varphi_{i}},\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}b=\begin{cases}\widetilde{F}_{i}b&\text{ if }|s_{i}|\leq\varphi_{i}\and\overline{s_{i}}\neq\overline{\varphi_{i}},\\
\operatorname{sgn}(s_{i})b&\text{ if }|s_{i}|>\varphi_{i},\\
\widetilde{E}_{i}b&\text{ if }|s_{i}|\leq\varphi_{i}\and\overline{s_{i}}=\overline{\varphi_{i}},\end{cases}\end{split}

(3)

If
a_{i,\tau(i)}=0
, then

\displaystyle\begin{split}\beta_{i}(b)&=\max(\varphi_{i},0,\varphi_{\tau(i)})-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}\\
&=\begin{cases}\varphi_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}>0,\varphi_{\tau(i)},\\
-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}\leq 0>\varphi_{\tau(i)},\\
\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}&\text{ if }\varphi_{i},0\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)},\end{cases}\\
\widetilde{B}_{i}b&=\begin{cases}\widetilde{F}_{i}b&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}>0,\varphi_{\tau(i)},\\
0&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}\leq 0>\varphi_{\tau(i)},\\
\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b&\text{ if }\varphi_{i},0\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)}.\end{cases}\end{split}

(4)

If
a_{i,\tau(i)}=-1
, then

\displaystyle\begin{split}\beta_{i}(b)&=\max(\varphi_{i},\max(0,s_{i}),\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i})-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)},\\
&=\begin{cases}\varphi_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}>\max(s_{i},0),\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i},\\
\max(-s_{i},0)-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}\leq\max(s_{i},0)>\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i},\\
\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}&\text{ if }\varphi_{i},\max(s_{i},0)\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i}.\end{cases}\end{split}

(a)

When
\varphi_{i}>\max(s_{i},0),\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i}
,

\widetilde{B}_{i}(b)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\widetilde{F}_{i}b&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}=\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i}+1\and\varphi_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{F}_{i}b)=\varphi_{\tau(i)}+1,\\
\widetilde{F}_{i}b&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

(b)

When
\varphi_{i}\leq\max(s_{i},0)>\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i}
,

\widetilde{B}_{i}b=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\widetilde{F}_{i}b&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}=s_{i}>0,\\
0&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

(c)

When
\varphi_{i},\max(s_{i},0)\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i}
,

\widetilde{B}_{i}b=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}=\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i}\and\varphi_{i}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b)=\varphi_{i},\\
&\text{ or }\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i}=0\geq s_{i}\and\varphi_{i}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b)<0,\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b+\widetilde{F}_{i}b)&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}=\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i}>\max(0,-s_{\tau(i)})\and\varphi_{i}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b)=\varphi_{i}-1,\\
\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Recall from Example
3.3.10
(
1
) that the crystal
\mathcal{B}(0)=\{b_{0}\}
has an
\imath
crystal structure. By Proposition
5.1.1
, the tensor product
\mathcal{B}(0)\otimes\mathcal{B}
has an
\imath
crystal structure. For each
b\in\mathcal{B}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}F_{i}(b_{0}\otimes b)&=\begin{cases}\varphi_{i}+\delta_{\overline{s_{i}+1},\varphi_{i}}&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}=2,\\
\varphi_{i}&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}\neq 2,\end{cases}\\
B_{i}(b_{0}\otimes b)&=\begin{cases}|s_{i}|&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}=2,\\
\max(0,s_{i})&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}\neq 2,\end{cases}\\
E_{i}(b_{0}\otimes b)&=\begin{cases}\varphi_{i}&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}=2,\\
\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i}&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}\neq 2.\end{cases}\end{split}

Now, the assertion follows from Proposition
5.1.1
by identifying
b_{0}\otimes b
with
b
.
∎

Corollary 5.2.2
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a seminormal crystal satisfying conditions
(S1)
–
(S3)’
for all
i,\tau(i)\in I
with
a_{i,\tau(i)}\neq 2
. For each
b\in\mathcal{B}
and
i\in I
, set
\varphi_{i}:=\varphi_{i}(b)
,
\varepsilon_{i}:=\varepsilon_{i}(b)
,
\operatorname{wt}:=\operatorname{wt}(b)
, and
\operatorname{wt}_{i}:=\operatorname{wt}_{i}(b)
. Then, it has an
\imath
crystal structure as follows: Let
b\in\mathcal{B}
and
i\in I
.

(1)

\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}(b)=\overline{\operatorname{wt}}
.

(2)

If
a_{i,\tau(i)}=2
, then

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b)=\begin{cases}\varepsilon_{i}+1&\text{ if }|s_{i}|\leq\varphi_{i}\and\overline{s_{i}}\neq\overline{\varphi_{i}},\\
|s_{i}|-\operatorname{wt}_{i}&\text{ if }|s_{i}|>\varphi_{i},\\
\varepsilon_{i}&\text{ if }|s_{i}|\leq\varphi_{i}\and\overline{s_{i}}=\overline{\varphi_{i}},\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}b=\begin{cases}\widetilde{F}_{i}b&\text{ if }|s_{i}|\leq\varphi_{i}\and\overline{s_{i}}\neq\overline{\varphi_{i}},\\
\operatorname{sgn}(s_{i})b&\text{ if }|s_{i}|>\varphi_{i},\\
\widetilde{E}_{i}b&\text{ if }|s_{i}|\leq\varphi_{i}\and\overline{s_{i}}=\overline{\varphi_{i}},\end{cases}\end{split}

(3)

If
a_{i,\tau(i)}=0
, then

\displaystyle\begin{split}\beta_{i}(b)&=\max(\varphi_{i},\varphi_{\tau(i)})-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}\\
&=\begin{cases}\varphi_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}>\varphi_{\tau(i)},\\
\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)},\end{cases}\\
\widetilde{B}_{i}b&=\begin{cases}\widetilde{F}_{i}b&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}>\varphi_{\tau(i)},\\
\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)}.\end{cases}\end{split}

(4)

If
a_{i,\tau(i)}=-1
, then

\displaystyle\begin{split}\beta_{i}(b)&=\max(\varphi_{i},\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i})-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)},\\
&=\begin{cases}\varphi_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}>\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i},\\
\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i}.\end{cases}\end{split}

(a)

When
\varphi_{i}>\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i}
,

\widetilde{B}_{i}(b)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\widetilde{F}_{i}b&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}=\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i}+1\and\varphi_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{F}_{i}b)=\varphi_{\tau(i)}+1,\\
\widetilde{F}_{i}b&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

(b)

When
\varphi_{i}\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i}
,

\widetilde{B}_{i}b=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}=\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i}\and\varphi_{i}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b)=\varphi_{i},\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b+\widetilde{F}_{i}b)&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}=\varphi_{\tau(i)}+s_{i}>\max(0,-s_{\tau(i)})\and\varphi_{i}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b)=\varphi_{i}-1,\\
\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Noting that we have
\varphi_{i}(b)\geq 0
for all
i\in I
, the assertion is immediate from Corollary
5.2.1
.
∎

Theorem 5.2.3
.

Let
M
be an integrable
\mathbf{U}
-module with a crystal base
(\mathcal{L}_{M},\mathcal{B}_{M})
. Then, as a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module, it satisfies the conditions
(C1)
–
(C3)
in the beginning of Section
3
, and the pair
(\mathcal{L}_{M},\mathcal{B}_{M})
is an
\imath
crystal base of
M
such that the
\imath
crystal structure of
\mathcal{B}_{M}
is given by Corollary
5.2.2
.

Proof.

By the definition of
\imath
crystal base (Definition
4.0.1
), it suffices to show that for each
i\in I
, the pair
(\mathcal{L}_{M},\mathcal{B}_{M})
is an
\imath
crystal base of
M
, regarded as a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{i}
-module.

First, we prove the assertion for the trivial module
V(0)=\mathbb{K}v_{0}
with crystal base
(\mathcal{L}(0)=\mathbb{K}_{\infty}v_{0},\mathcal{B}=\{b_{0}\})
, where
b_{0}:=\mathrm{ev}_{\infty}(v_{0})
. By Lemmas
4.1.1
,
4.2.2
, and
4.3.3
, we have

\beta_{i}(b_{0})=\begin{cases}|s_{i}|&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}=2,\\
\max(-s_{i},0)&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}\neq 2,\end{cases}\quad\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{0}=\begin{cases}\operatorname{sgn}(s_{i})b_{0}&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}=2,\\
0&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}\neq 2.\end{cases}

This shows that
\mathcal{B}(0)
is an
\imath
crystal basis of
V(0)
whose
\imath
crystal structure is as in Example
3.3.10
(
1
).

Next, let us investigate how
\beta_{i}
and
\widetilde{B}_{i}
act on
M\simeq V(0)\otimes M
for each
i\in I
. Since
M
is integrable, as a
\mathbf{U}_{i,\tau(i)}
-module, it can be embedded into a direct sum of tensor powers
V_{\natural}^{\otimes N}
of the natural representation of
\mathbf{U}_{i,\tau(i)}
for various
N\geq 0
. Therefore, we may assume that
M\simeq V_{\natural}^{\otimes N}
for some
N>0
as a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}_{i}
-module. Now, the assertion follows from Remark
5.1.4
.
∎

Proposition 5.2.4
.

Let
\mathcal{B}_{1},\mathcal{B}_{3}
be
\imath
crystals,
\mathcal{B}_{2},\mathcal{B}_{4}
crystals,
\mu_{1}:\mathcal{B}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}_{3}
an
\imath
crystal morphisms,
\mu_{2}:\mathcal{B}_{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}_{4}
a crystal morphism. Let
\mu_{1}\otimes\mu_{2}:\mathbb{C}\mathcal{B}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{C}\mathcal{B}_{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\mathcal{B}_{3}\otimes\mathbb{C}\mathcal{B}_{4}
denote the
\mathbb{C}
-linear map given by
(\mu_{1}\otimes\mu_{2})(b_{1}\otimes b_{2})=\mu_{1}(b_{1})\otimes\mu_{2}(b_{2})
. Then, the following hold:

(1)

\mu_{1}\otimes\mu_{2}
is an
\imath
crystal morphism.

(2)

If
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
are strict, then so is
\mu_{1}\otimes\mu_{2}
.

(3)

If
\mu_{1}
is very strict and
\mu_{2}
is strict, then
\mu_{1}\otimes\mu_{2}
is very strict.

(4)

If
\mu_{1}
is an equivalence and
\mu_{2}
is an isomorphism, then
\mu_{1}\otimes\mu_{2}
is an equivalence.

(5)

If
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
are isomorphisms, then so is
\mu_{1}\otimes\mu_{2}
.

Proof.

First, we confirm Definition
3.3.8
(
1
). Let
b_{i}\in\mathcal{B}_{i}
be such that
(\mu_{1}(b_{1})\otimes\mu_{2}(b_{2}),b_{3}\otimes b_{4})\neq 0
. Then, we have
(\mu_{1}(b_{1}),b_{3})\neq 0
and
\mu_{2}(b_{2})=b_{4}
. Setting
b:=b_{1}\otimes b_{2}
and
b^{\prime}:=b_{3}\otimes b_{4}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}(b_{3}\otimes b_{4})&=\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}(b_{3})+\overline{\operatorname{wt}(b_{4})}=\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}(b_{1})+\overline{\operatorname{wt}(b_{2})}=\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}(b_{1}\otimes b_{2}),\\
\beta_{i}(b_{3}\otimes b_{4})&=\begin{cases}\max(F_{i}(b^{\prime}),B_{i}(b^{\prime}),E_{i}(b^{\prime}))-\operatorname{wt}_{i}(b_{4})&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}=2,\\
\max(F_{i}(b^{\prime}),B_{i}(b^{\prime}),E_{i}(b^{\prime}))+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b_{3})-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}(b_{4})&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}\neq 2\end{cases}\\
&=\begin{cases}\max(F_{i}(b),B_{i}(b),E_{i}(b))-\operatorname{wt}_{i}(b_{2})&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}=2,\\
\max(F_{i}(b),B_{i}(b),E_{i}(b))+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b_{1})-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}(b_{2})&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}\neq 2\end{cases}\\
&=\beta_{i}(b_{1}\otimes b_{2}).\end{split}

This confirms Definition
3.3.8
(
1
).

Next, let us confirm Definition
3.3.8
(
2
). Suppose that
\widetilde{B}_{i}(b_{1}\otimes b_{2})\in\mathcal{B}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{2}
. Then, we have

\widetilde{B}_{i}(b_{1}\otimes b_{2})=\begin{cases}b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)>B_{i}(b),E_{i}(b),\\
\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\otimes b_{2}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)\leq B_{i}(b)>E_{i}(b),\\
b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b),B_{i}(b)\leq E_{i}(b),\end{cases}

We claim that

\widetilde{B}_{i}(b_{3}\otimes b_{4})=\begin{cases}b_{3}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{4}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)>B_{i}(b),E_{i}(b),\\
\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{3}\otimes b_{4}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)\leq B_{i}(b)>E_{i}(b),\\
b_{3}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{4}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b),B_{i}(b)\leq E_{i}(b).\end{cases}

First, consider the case when
F_{i}(b^{\prime})>B_{i}(b^{\prime}),E_{i}(b^{\prime})
. If our claim fails, then we have
F_{i}(b^{\prime})=E_{i}(b^{\prime})+1
and
\varphi_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{4})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b_{4})+1
. Since
\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2}\neq 0
, we have
\mu_{2}(\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2})=\widetilde{F}_{i}\mu_{2}(b_{2})=\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{4}
, and hence,

\varphi_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{4})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b_{4})+1=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b_{2})+1.

This, together with
F_{i}(b)=F_{i}(b^{\prime})=E_{i}(b^{\prime})+1=E_{i}(b)+1
implies that
\widetilde{B}_{i}b=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2}
, which is a contradiction. Thus, we obtain
\widetilde{B}_{i}b^{\prime}=b_{3}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{4}
. By the same way, our claim follows in the case when
F_{i}(b^{\prime}),B_{i}(b^{\prime})\leq E_{i}(b^{\prime})
.

Next, consider the case when
F_{i}(b^{\prime})\leq B_{i}(b^{\prime})>E_{i}(b^{\prime})
. Since
\widetilde{B}_{i}b\in\mathcal{B}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{2}
, we have
\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{1}
. If it holds that
F_{i}(b^{\prime})=B_{i}(b^{\prime})\neq\beta_{\tau(i)}(b_{3})
, then we obtain
F_{i}(b)=B_{i}(b)\neq\beta_{\tau(i)}(b_{1})
, and hence,
\widetilde{B}_{i}b=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\otimes b_{2}+b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2})
. This is a contradiction. On the other hand, if
B_{i}(b^{\prime})=E_{i}(b^{\prime})+1
and
\beta_{i}(\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{3})=\beta_{i}(b_{3})-2
, then it follows that
\mu_{1}(\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1})=\widetilde{B}_{i}\mu_{1}(b_{1})=\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{3}
. Therefore, we have
B_{i}(b)=E_{i}(b)+1
and
\beta_{i}(\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1})=\beta_{i}(b_{1})-2
, and hence,
\widetilde{B}_{i}b=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\otimes b_{2}
. This causes a contradiction, too. Thus, we obtain
\widetilde{B}_{i}b^{\prime}=\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{3}\otimes b_{4}
.

Now, we compute as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widetilde{B}_{i}(\mu_{1}(b_{1})\otimes\mu_{2}(b_{2}))&=\sum_{b_{3}\in\mathcal{B}_{3}}(\mu_{1}(b_{1}),b_{3})\widetilde{B}_{i}(b_{3}\otimes b_{4})\\
&=\begin{cases}\sum_{b_{3}\in\mathcal{B}_{3}}(\mu_{1}(b_{1}),b_{3})b_{3}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{4}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)>B_{i}(b),E_{i}(b),\\
\sum_{b_{3}\in\mathcal{B}_{3}}(\mu_{1}(b_{1}),b_{3})\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{3}\otimes b_{4}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)\leq B_{i}(b)>E_{i}(b),\\
\sum_{b_{3}\in\mathcal{B}_{3}}(\mu_{1}(b_{1}),b_{3})b_{3}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{4}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b),B_{i}(b)\leq E_{i}(b)\end{cases}\\
&=\begin{cases}\mu_{1}(b_{1})\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{4}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)>B_{i}(b),E_{i}(b),\\
\widetilde{B}_{i}\mu_{1}(b_{1})\otimes b_{4}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b)\leq B_{i}(b)>E_{i}(b),\\
\mu_{1}(b_{1})\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{4}&\text{ if }F_{i}(b),B_{i}(b)\leq E_{i}(b)\end{cases}\\
&=(\mu_{1}\otimes\mu_{2})(\widetilde{B}_{i}(b_{1}\otimes b_{2})).\end{split}

This confirms Definition
3.3.8
(
2
). Thus, the first assertion of the proposition follows.

Let us prove the second assertion. By the first assertion, it suffices to show that
\widetilde{B}_{i}(\mu_{1}(b_{1})\otimes\mu_{2}(b_{2}))=(\mu_{1}\otimes\mu_{2})(\widetilde{B}_{i}(b_{1}\otimes b_{2}))
for all
b_{1}\otimes b_{2}\in\mathcal{B}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{2}
and
i\in I
. Let
b_{3}\otimes b_{4}\in\mathcal{B}_{3}\otimes\mathcal{B}_{4}
be such that
(\mu_{1}(b_{1})\otimes\mu_{2}(b_{2}),b_{3}\otimes b_{4})\neq 0
. By the definition of
\widetilde{B}_{i}
on tensor products, and the strictness of
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widetilde{B}_{i}(b_{1}\otimes b_{2})=c_{F}b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2}+c_{B}\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\otimes b_{2}+c_{E}b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2},\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}(b_{3}\otimes b_{4})=c_{F}b_{3}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{4}+c_{B}\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{3}\otimes b_{4}+c_{E}b_{3}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{4}\end{split}

for some
c_{F},c_{B},c_{E}\in\mathbb{C}
. Therefore, we compute

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widetilde{B}_{i}(\mu_{1}(b_{1})\otimes\mu_{2}(b_{2}))&=\sum_{b_{3}\in\mathcal{B}_{3}}(\mu_{1}(b_{1}),b_{3})\widetilde{B}_{i}(b_{3}\otimes b_{4})\\
&=\sum_{b_{3}\in\mathcal{B}_{3}}(\mu_{1}(b_{1}),b_{3})(c_{F}b_{3}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{4}+c_{B}\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{3}\otimes b_{4}+c_{E}b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{4})\\
&=c_{F}\mu_{1}(b_{1})\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{4}+c_{B}\widetilde{B}_{i}\mu_{1}(b_{1})\otimes b_{4}+c_{E}\mu_{1}(b_{1})\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{4}\\
&=c_{F}\mu_{1}(b_{1})\otimes\mu_{2}(\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2})+c_{B}\mu_{1}(\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1})\otimes\mu_{2}(b_{2})+c_{E}\mu_{1}(b_{1})\otimes\mu_{2}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2})\\
&=(\mu_{1}\otimes\mu_{2})(\widetilde{B}_{i}(b_{1}\otimes b_{2})).\end{split}

This proves the second assertion. The remaining assertions are now obvious. Thus, the proof completes.
∎

6.
\imath
Crystal basis of the modified
\imath
quantum group

In this section, we construct projective systems of
\imath
crystals and very strict morphisms, and describe their projective limits explicitly. Then, we lift this result to based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-modules.

6.1.
\imath
Crystal basis of
\dot{\mathbf{U}}^{\imath}

For the rest of this paper, we fix
\sigma\in X^{+}
such that

\langle h_{i},\sigma\rangle=\begin{cases}|s_{i}|&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}=2,\\
0&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}=0,\\
\max(s_{i},0)&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}=-1\and i\in I_{\tau},\\
\max(-s_{\tau(i)},0)&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}=-1\and i\notin I_{\tau}.\end{cases}

Note that we have

\langle h_{i}-h_{\tau(i)},\sigma\rangle=\begin{cases}s_{i}&\text{ if }i\in I_{\tau},\\
-s_{\tau(i)}&\text{ if }i\notin I_{\tau}\end{cases}

for all
i\in I
with
a_{i,\tau(i)}=-1
.

Recall from Section
3
the
1
-dimensional
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module
V(0)^{\sigma}=\mathbb{K}v_{0}^{\sigma}
and its
\mathbb{K}_{\infty}
-lattice
\mathcal{L}(0)^{\sigma}=\mathbb{K}_{\infty}v_{0}^{\sigma}
. Set
b_{0}^{\sigma}:=\mathrm{ev}_{\infty}(v_{0}^{\sigma})
,
\mathcal{B}(0)^{\sigma}:=\{b_{0}^{\sigma}\}
.

Lemma 6.1.1
.

The pair
(\mathcal{L}(0)^{\sigma},\mathcal{B}(0)^{\sigma})
is an
\imath
crystal base of
V(0)^{\sigma}
; its
\imath
crystal structure is given as follows:

\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}(b_{0}^{\sigma})=\overline{\sigma},\quad\beta_{i}(b_{0}^{\sigma})=0,\quad\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{0}^{\sigma}=0.

Proof.

The assertion is clear from the
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module structure of
V(0)^{\sigma}
.
∎

Lemma 6.1.2
.

Let
\nu\in X^{+}
. Then, there exists a very strict
\imath
crystal morphism
\gamma_{\nu}:\mathcal{B}(\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu))\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(0)^{\sigma}
such that

\gamma_{\nu}(b)=\delta_{b,b_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)}}b_{0}^{\sigma}.

for all
b\in\mathcal{B}(\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu))
.

Proof.

By Corollary
5.2.2
, we have

\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}(b_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)})=\overline{\sigma},\quad\beta_{i}(b_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)})=0,\quad\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)}=0.

This, together with Lemma
6.1.1
, shows that
\{b_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)}\}
forms an
\imath
crystal isomorphic to
\mathcal{B}(0)^{\sigma}
. Thus, the assertion follows.
∎

Recall that for each
\lambda\in X^{+}
, we set

V(\lambda)^{\sigma}:=V(0)^{\sigma}\otimes V(\lambda),\quad\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma}:=\mathcal{B}(0)^{\sigma}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\lambda),

and

v^{\sigma}:=v_{0}^{\sigma}\otimes v,\quad b^{\sigma}:=b_{0}^{\sigma}\otimes b

for all
v\in V(\lambda)
and
b\in\mathcal{B}(\lambda)
.

Lemma 6.1.3
.

Let
\lambda\in X^{+}
. Then, there exists a very strict
\imath
crystal morphism
\rho_{\lambda}:\mathcal{B}(\sigma+\lambda)\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma}
such that

\rho_{\lambda}(\pi_{\sigma+\lambda}(b))=\pi_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma}

for all
b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty)
. Consequently, there exists an injective very strict
\imath
crystal morphism
\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{B}(\sigma+\lambda)
which sends
\pi_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma}
to
\pi_{\sigma+\lambda}(b)
for all
b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty;\lambda)
.

Proof.

By
[
14
, Proposition 25.1.2]
, there exists an injective strict crystal morphism
\eta_{\sigma,\lambda}:\mathcal{B}(\sigma+\lambda)\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\sigma)\otimes\mathcal{B}(\lambda)
such that

\eta_{\sigma,\lambda}(\pi_{\sigma+\lambda}(b))=b_{\sigma}\otimes\pi_{\lambda}(b)\quad\text{ for all }b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty;\lambda),

and

\eta_{\sigma,\lambda}(\pi_{\sigma+\lambda}(b))\notin b_{\sigma}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\lambda)\quad\text{ for all }b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty){\setminus}\mathcal{B}(\infty;\lambda).

By Lemma
6.1.2
and Proposition
5.2.4
, there exists a very strict
\imath
crystal morphism

\rho_{\lambda}:\mathcal{B}(\sigma+\lambda)\xrightarrow{\eta_{\sigma,\lambda}}\mathcal{B}(\sigma)\otimes\mathcal{B}(\lambda)\xrightarrow{\gamma_{0}\otimes 1}\mathcal{B}(0)^{\sigma}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\lambda)=\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma}.

Then, it is clear that this morphism has the required property.
∎

Proposition 6.1.4
.

Let
\lambda,\nu\in X^{+}
. Then, there exists a very strict
\imath
crystal morphism

\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}:\mathcal{B}(\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu))^{\sigma}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma}

such that

\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}(\pi_{\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(b)^{\sigma})=\pi_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma}

for all
b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty)
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
6.1.3
, there exists a very strict
\imath
crystal morphism

\mathcal{B}(\sigma+\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu))\xrightarrow{\eta_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu),\lambda}}\mathcal{B}(\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu))\otimes\mathcal{B}(\lambda)\xrightarrow{\gamma_{\nu}\otimes 1}\mathcal{B}(0)^{\sigma}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\lambda)=\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma}

which sends
\pi_{\sigma+\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(b)
to
\pi_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma}
. On the other hand, by Lemma
6.1.3
, we have a very strict
\imath
crystal morphism

\mathcal{B}(\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu))^{\sigma}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{B}(\sigma+\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)).

Combining these two morphisms, we obtain a very strict
\imath
crystal morphism

\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}:\mathcal{B}(\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu))^{\sigma}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma}.

Then, it is clear that this morphism satisfies the required property.
∎

Now, for each
\zeta\in X^{\imath}
, we obtain a projective system
\{\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma}\}_{\lambda\in X^{+},\overline{\lambda}=\zeta}
of
\imath
crystals with very strict morphisms
\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}:\mathcal{B}(\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu))^{\sigma}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma}
. We shall describe its projective limit in the category of
\imath
crystals and very strict morphisms. To do so, we prepare three lemmas.

Lemma 6.1.5
.

Let
\lambda\in X^{+}
. Then, there exists an
\imath
crystal isomorphism
\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\lambda)
which sends
b^{\sigma}
to
t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes b
for all
b\in\mathcal{B}(\lambda)
.

Proof.

Let us compare the
\imath
crystal structures of
\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma}
and
\mathcal{T}_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\lambda)
. To do so, recall from Lemma
2.2.9
(
4
) that
\varphi_{i}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b)=\varphi_{i}(b)-1
is equivalent to
\varphi_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{F}_{i}b)=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)
. Then, the the
\imath
crystal structure of
\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma}
is described as follows: Let
b\in\mathcal{B}(\lambda)
and
i\in I
.

(1)

\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}(b^{\sigma})=\overline{\sigma}+\overline{\operatorname{wt}(b)}
.

(2)

When
a_{i,\tau(i)}=2
.

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b^{\sigma})=\begin{cases}\varepsilon_{i}(b)+1&\text{ if }\overline{\varphi_{i}(b)}=\overline{1},\\
\varepsilon_{i}(b)&\text{ if }\overline{\varphi_{i}(b)}=\overline{0},\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}b^{\sigma}=\begin{cases}(\widetilde{F}_{i}b)^{\sigma}&\text{ if }\overline{\varphi_{i}(b)}=\overline{1},\\
(\widetilde{E}_{i}b)^{\sigma}&\text{ if }\overline{\varphi_{i}(b)}=\overline{0}.\end{cases}\end{split}

(3)

When
a_{i,\tau(i)}=0
.

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b^{\sigma})=\begin{cases}\varphi_{i}(b)-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}(b)&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}(b)>\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b),\\
\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}(b)&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}(b)\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b),\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}b^{\sigma}=\begin{cases}(\widetilde{F}_{i}b)^{\sigma}&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}(b)>\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b),\\
(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b)^{\sigma}&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}(b)\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b).\end{cases}\end{split}

(4)

When
a_{i,\tau(i)}=-1
. Setting
s^{\prime}_{i}:=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }i\in I_{\tau},\\
1&\text{ if }i\notin I_{\tau},\end{cases}
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F_{i}(b^{\sigma})=\varphi_{i}(b),\\
&B_{i}(b^{\sigma})=s^{\prime}_{i},\\
&E_{i}(b^{\sigma})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)+s^{\prime}_{i}.\end{split}

This implies that
B_{i}(b^{\sigma})\leq E_{i}(b^{\sigma})
. Therefore, we obtain the following:

(a)

When
\varphi_{i}(b)>\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)+s^{\prime}_{i}
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\beta_{i}(b^{\sigma})&=\varphi_{i}(b)-s^{\prime}_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}(b),\\
\widetilde{B}_{i}b^{\sigma}&=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\widetilde{F}_{i}b)^{\sigma}&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}(b)=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)+s^{\prime}_{i}+1\and\varphi_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{F}_{i}b)=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)+1,\\
(\widetilde{F}_{i}b)^{\sigma}&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}\end{split}

(b)

When
\varphi_{i}(b)\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)+s^{\prime}_{i}
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\beta_{i}(b^{\sigma})&=\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}(b),\\
\widetilde{B}_{i}b^{\sigma}&=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b)^{\sigma}&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}(b)=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)+s^{\prime}_{i}\and\varphi_{i}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b)=\varphi_{i}(b),\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}((\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b)^{\sigma}+(\widetilde{F}_{i}b)^{\sigma})&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}(b)=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)+s^{\prime}_{i}>0\and\varphi_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{F}_{i}b)=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b),\\
(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b)^{\sigma}&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}\end{split}

On the other hand, the
\imath
crystal structure of
\mathcal{T}_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\lambda)
is described as follows:

(1)

\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}(t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes b)=\overline{\sigma}+\overline{\operatorname{wt}(b)}
.

(2)

When
a_{i,\tau(i)}=2
.

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes b)=\begin{cases}\varepsilon_{i}(b)+1&\text{ if }\overline{\varphi_{i}(b)}=\overline{1},\\
\varepsilon_{i}(b)&\text{ if }\overline{\varphi_{i}(b)}=\overline{0},\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes b=\begin{cases}t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b&\text{ if }\overline{\varphi_{i}(b)}=\overline{1},\\
t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{i}b&\text{ if }\overline{\varphi_{i}(b)}=\overline{0}.\end{cases}\end{split}

(3)

When
a_{i,\tau(i)}=0
.

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes b)=\begin{cases}\varphi_{i}(b)-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}(b)&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}(b)>\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b),\\
\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}(b)&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}(b)\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b),\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes b=\begin{cases}t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}(b)>\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b),\\
t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}(b)\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b).\end{cases}\end{split}

(4)

When
a_{i,\tau(i)}=-1
. Setting
s^{\prime}_{i}:=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }i\in I_{\tau},\\
1&\text{ if }i\notin I_{\tau},\end{cases}

we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F_{i}(t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes b)=\varphi_{i}(b),\\
&B_{i}(t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes b)=-\infty,\\
&E_{i}(t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes b)=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)+s^{\prime}_{i}.\end{split}

This implies that
B_{i}(t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes b)<E_{i}(t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes b)
. Therefore, we obtain the following:

(a)

When
\varphi_{i}(b)>\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)+s^{\prime}_{i}
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\beta_{i}(t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes b)&=\varphi_{i}(b)-s^{\prime}_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}(b),\\
\widetilde{B}_{i}(t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes b)&=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}(b)=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)+s^{\prime}_{i}+1\and\varphi_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{F}_{i}b)=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)+1,\\
t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}\end{split}

(b)

When
\varphi_{i}(b)\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)+s^{\prime}_{i}
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\beta_{i}(t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes b)&=\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}(b),\\
\widetilde{B}_{i}(t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes b)&=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}(b)=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)+s^{\prime}_{i}\and\varphi_{i}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b)=\varphi_{i}(b),\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b+\widetilde{F}_{i}b)&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}(b)=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)+s^{\prime}_{i}\and\varphi_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b),\\
t_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}\end{split}

Thus, the proof completes (note that
\varphi_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)
implies that
\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2}\in\mathcal{B}(\lambda)
, and hence,
\varphi_{i}(b)>0
).
∎

Lemma 6.1.6
.

Let
\zeta\in X^{\imath}
and
\mu\in X
. Then, we have an
\imath
crystal isomorphism
\mathcal{T}_{\zeta}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{\mu}\simeq\mathcal{T}_{\zeta+\overline{\mu}}
.

Proof.

For each
i\in I
with
a_{i,\tau(i)}=2
, let
\zeta_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
denote the value of
\zeta
at
i
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}(t_{\zeta}\otimes t_{\mu})=\zeta+\overline{\mu},\\
&\beta_{i}(t_{\zeta}\otimes t_{\mu})=\begin{cases}-\infty_{\mathrm{ev}}&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}=2\and\zeta_{i}+\overline{\langle h_{i},\mu\rangle}=\overline{s_{i}},\\
-\infty_{\mathrm{odd}}&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}=2\and\zeta_{i}+\overline{\langle h_{i},\mu\rangle}\neq\overline{s_{i}},\\
-\infty&\text{ if }a_{i,\tau(i)}\neq 2,\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}(t_{\zeta}\otimes t_{\mu})=0.\end{split}

This datum coincides with that of
t_{\zeta+\overline{\mu}}\in\mathcal{T}_{\zeta+\overline{\mu}}
. Hence, the assertion follows.
∎

Lemma 6.1.7
.

Let
\lambda\in X^{+}
. Then, there exists an injective very strict
\imath
crystal morphism
\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\overline{\sigma+\lambda}}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)
which sends
\pi_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma}
to
t_{\overline{\sigma+\lambda}}\otimes b
for all
b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty;\lambda)
.

Proof.

By Lemmas
6.1.5
and
6.1.6
, we have an injective
\imath
crystal morphism

\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma}\simeq\mathcal{T}_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\lambda)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\overline{\sigma}}\otimes(\mathcal{T}_{\lambda}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty))\simeq\mathcal{T}_{\overline{\sigma+\lambda}}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)

which sends
\pi_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma}
to
t_{\overline{\sigma+\lambda}}\otimes b
for all
b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty;\lambda)
. Hence, to prove the assertion, it suffices to show that for each
i\in I
,
b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty;\lambda)
, and
b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}(\infty)
with
(\widetilde{B}_{i}(t_{\overline{\sigma+\lambda}}\otimes b),t_{\overline{\sigma+\lambda}}\otimes b^{\prime})\neq 0
, we have
b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}(\infty;\lambda)
.

Let
i\in I
,
b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty;\lambda)
. Since there are at most two
b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}(\infty)
satisfying
(\widetilde{B}_{i}(t_{\overline{\sigma+\lambda}}\otimes b),t_{\overline{\sigma+\lambda}}\otimes b^{\prime})\neq 0
, we may take
\nu\in X^{+}
such that
b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}(\infty;\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu))
for all such
b^{\prime}
.

As before, we have an injective
\imath
crystal morphism

\mathcal{B}(\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu))^{\sigma}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\overline{\sigma+\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)=\mathcal{T}_{\overline{\sigma+\lambda}}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty).

Therefore, if we write
\widetilde{B}_{i}(t_{\overline{\sigma+\lambda}}\otimes b)=\sum_{b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}(\infty;\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu))}c_{b^{\prime}}t_{\overline{\sigma+\lambda}}\otimes b^{\prime}
for some
c_{b^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{C}
, we obtain

\widetilde{B}_{i}\pi_{\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(b)^{\sigma}=\sum_{b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}(\infty;\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu))}c_{b^{\prime}}\pi_{\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(b^{\prime})^{\sigma}.

On the other hand, by Lemma
6.1.4
, we have an injective very strict
\imath
crystal morphism

\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{B}(\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu))^{\sigma}

whose image is
\{b^{\sigma}\mid b\in\mathcal{B}(\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu);\lambda)\}
. Therefore, we obtain
\pi_{\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(b^{\prime})\in\mathcal{B}(\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu);\lambda)
for all
b^{\prime}
with
c_{b^{\prime}}\neq 0
. In other words, we have
b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}(\infty;\lambda)
for all
b^{\prime}
with
c_{b^{\prime}}\neq 0
. This proves our claim, and hence, the assertion follows.
∎

Theorem 6.1.8
.

Let
\zeta\in X^{\imath}
. For each
\lambda\in X^{+}
such that
\overline{\sigma+\lambda}=\zeta
, there exists a very strict
\imath
crystal morphism

\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{T}_{\zeta}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma};\ t_{\zeta}\otimes b\mapsto\pi_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma}.

Moreover,
\mathcal{T}_{\zeta}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)
and
\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda}
’s form the projective limit of
\{\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma}\}_{\lambda\in X^{+},\overline{\sigma+\lambda}=\zeta}
in the category of
\imath
crystals and very strict morphisms.

Proof.

Let
\lambda\in X^{+}
be such that
\overline{\sigma+\lambda}=\zeta
, and
b\in\mathcal{B}(\lambda)
. By Lemma
6.1.7
, we have an injective very strict
\imath
crystal morphism

\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\zeta}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)

which sends
\pi_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma}
to
t_{\zeta}\otimes b
for all
b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty;\lambda)
. Therefore, there exists a very strict
\imath
crystal morphism

\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{T}_{\zeta}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma};\ t_{\zeta}\otimes b\mapsto\pi_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma}.

Then, we have

\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}\circ\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}=\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda}

for all
\nu\in X^{+}
.

Let us prove the universality. Let
\mathcal{B}
be an
\imath
crystal and
\mu_{\lambda}:\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma}
a very strict
\imath
crystal morphism such that
\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}\circ\mu_{\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}=\mu_{\lambda}
for all
\lambda,\nu\in X^{+}
with
\overline{\sigma+\lambda}=\zeta
. Define a map
\mu:\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\zeta}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)
as follows. Let
b\in\mathcal{B}
. If
\mu_{\lambda}(b)=0
for all
\lambda\in X^{+}
with
\overline{\sigma+\lambda}=\zeta
, then set
\mu(b):=0
. If
\mu_{\lambda}(b)=m_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma}
for some
\lambda\in X^{+}
with
\overline{\sigma+\lambda}=\zeta
and
m_{\lambda}(b)\in\mathcal{B}(\lambda)
, then set
\mu(b):=t_{\zeta}\otimes m(b)
, where
m(b)\in\mathcal{B}(\infty)
is such that
\pi_{\lambda}(m(b))=m_{\lambda}(b)
. In order to see the well-definedness, let
\lambda^{\prime}\in X^{+}
be such that
\overline{\sigma+\lambda^{\prime}}=\zeta
and
\mu_{\lambda^{\prime}}(b)=m_{\lambda^{\prime}}(b)^{\sigma}
for some
m_{\lambda^{\prime}}(b)\in\mathcal{B}(\lambda^{\prime})
. Let
m^{\prime}(b)\in\mathcal{B}(\infty)
be such that
\pi_{\lambda^{\prime}}(m^{\prime}(b))=m_{\lambda^{\prime}}(b)
. We want to show that
m(b)=m^{\prime}(b)
. Since
\overline{\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}}=\overline{0}
, there exists
\nu,\nu^{\prime}\in X^{+}
such that
\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}=\nu^{\prime}+\tau(\nu^{\prime})-(\nu+\tau(\nu))
. Set
\lambda^{\prime\prime}:=\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)=\lambda^{\prime}+\nu^{\prime}+\tau(\nu^{\prime})
. Since

\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}(\mu_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}(b))=\mu_{\lambda}(b)=m_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma},

we see that
\mu_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}(b)\neq 0
. Hence, there exists
b^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{B}(\infty)
such that
\mu_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}(b)=\pi_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime})^{\sigma}
. This implies that

\pi_{\lambda}(b^{\prime\prime})^{\sigma}=\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}(\pi_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime})^{\sigma})=\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}(\mu_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}(b))=m_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma}=\pi_{\lambda}(m(b))^{\sigma}

and hence,

b^{\prime\prime}=m(b).

Similarly, we obtain
b^{\prime\prime}=m^{\prime}(b)
. Thus, we obtain
m(b)=m^{\prime}(b)
, as desired. Hence,
\mu
is well-defined.

For each
\lambda\in X^{+}
such that
\overline{\sigma+\lambda}=\zeta
, we have

\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda}(\mu(b))=\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda}(t_{\zeta}\otimes m(b))=\pi_{\lambda}(m(b))^{\sigma}=m_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma}=\mu_{\lambda}(b).

Here, we set
m(b)=0
if
\mu(b)=0
. Therefore, we obtain

\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda}\circ\mu=\mu_{\lambda}.

It remains to shows that
\mu
is a very strict
\imath
crystal morphism. Let
b\in\mathcal{B}
and
i\in I
. Let us write
\widetilde{B}_{i}b=\sum_{b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}}c_{b^{\prime}}b^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{B}_{i}\mu(b)=\sum_{b^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{B}(\infty)}d_{b^{\prime\prime}}t_{\zeta}\otimes b^{\prime\prime}
for some
c_{b^{\prime}},d_{b^{\prime\prime}}\in\mathbb{C}
. We can take
\lambda\in X^{+}
such that
\overline{\sigma+\lambda}=\zeta
and
\mu_{\lambda}(b^{\prime}),\pi_{\lambda}(b^{\prime\prime})\in\mathcal{B}(\lambda)
for all
b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}
and
b^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{B}(\infty)
with
c_{b^{\prime}},d_{b^{\prime\prime}}\neq 0
and
\mu(b^{\prime})\neq 0
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{b^{\prime}}\neq 0\\
\mu(b^{\prime})\neq 0\end{subarray}}c_{b^{\prime}}\mu_{\lambda}(b^{\prime})&=\mu_{\lambda}(\widetilde{B}_{i}b)=\widetilde{B}_{i}\mu_{\lambda}(b)=\widetilde{B}_{i}\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda}(\mu(b))=\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{B}_{i}\mu(b))=\sum_{d_{b^{\prime\prime}}\neq 0}d_{b^{\prime\prime}}\pi_{\lambda}(b^{\prime\prime})^{\sigma}.\end{split}

This implies that
c_{b^{\prime}}=d_{b^{\prime\prime}}
if
\mu_{\lambda}(b^{\prime})=\pi_{\lambda}(b^{\prime\prime})^{\sigma}
. Therefore, noting that
\mu_{\lambda}(b^{\prime})=\pi_{\lambda}(b^{\prime\prime})^{\sigma}
implies that
\mu(b^{\prime})=t_{\zeta}\otimes b^{\prime\prime}
, we obtain

\mu(\widetilde{B}_{i}b)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{b^{\prime}}\neq 0\\
\mu(b^{\prime})\neq 0\end{subarray}}c_{b^{\prime}}\mu(b^{\prime})=\sum_{d_{b^{\prime\prime}}\neq 0}d_{b^{\prime\prime}}t_{\zeta}\otimes b^{\prime\prime}=\widetilde{B}_{i}\mu(b).

Thus, the proof completes.
∎

Example 6.1.9
.

Suppose that our Satake diagram is of diagonal type. We retain notation in Examples
3.1.1
. Let us see the
\imath
crystal structure of
\mathcal{T}_{\zeta}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)
,
\zeta\in X^{\imath}
. For each
i\in I_{\tau}=I_{2}
and
b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty)
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F_{i}(t_{\zeta}\otimes b)=\varphi_{i}(b),\quad B_{i}(t_{\zeta}\otimes b)=-\infty,\quad E_{i}(t_{\zeta}\otimes b)=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)-\zeta_{i},\\
&F_{\tau(i)}(t_{\zeta}\otimes b)=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b),\quad B_{\tau(i)}(t_{\zeta}\otimes b)=-\infty,\quad E_{\tau(i)}(t_{\zeta}\otimes b)=\varphi_{i}(b)+\zeta_{i},\end{split}

where
\zeta_{i}:=\langle h_{i}-h_{\tau(i)},\zeta\rangle
. Then, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(t_{\zeta}\otimes b)=\max(\varphi_{i}(b)+\zeta_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}(b),\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}(b)),\\
&\beta_{\tau(i)}(t_{\zeta}\otimes b)=\max(\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)-\zeta_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{i}(b),\varepsilon_{i}(b)),\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}(t_{\zeta}\otimes b)=\begin{cases}t_{\zeta}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}(b)>\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)-\zeta_{i},\\
t_{\zeta}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b&\text{ if }\varphi_{i}(b)\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)-\zeta_{i},\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}(t_{\zeta}\otimes b)=\begin{cases}t_{\zeta}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{\tau(i)}b&\text{ if }\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)>\varphi_{i}(b)+\zeta_{i},\\
t_{\zeta}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{i}b&\text{ if }\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)\leq\varphi_{i}(b)+\zeta_{i}.\end{cases}\end{split}

Under the identification
\mathbf{U}\simeq\mathbf{U}_{I^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{I^{\prime}}
, the crystal
\mathcal{B}(\infty)
is identified with
\mathcal{B}(-\infty)_{I^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)_{I^{\prime}}
, where
\mathcal{B}(-\infty)_{I^{\prime}}
denotes the crystal basis of the positive part of
\mathbf{U}_{I^{\prime}}
. If we write
b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty)
as
b_{1}\otimes b_{2}\in\mathcal{B}(-\infty)_{I^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)_{I^{\prime}}
, the
\mathbf{U}
-crystal structure of
\mathcal{B}(\infty)
and the
\mathbf{U}_{I^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{I^{\prime}}
-crystal structure of
\mathcal{B}(\infty)=\mathcal{B}(-\infty)_{I^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)_{I^{\prime}}
are related as follows: Let
k\in I^{\prime}
.

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varphi_{k_{1}}(b)=\varepsilon_{k}(b_{1}),\quad\varphi_{k_{2}}(b)=\varphi_{k}(b_{2}),\quad\varepsilon_{k_{1}}(b)=\varphi_{k}(b_{1}),\quad\varepsilon_{k_{2}}(b)=\varepsilon_{k}(b_{2}),\\
&\widetilde{F}_{k_{1}}b=\widetilde{E}_{k}b_{1}\otimes b_{2},\quad\widetilde{F}_{k_{2}}b=b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{k}b_{2},\quad\widetilde{E}_{k_{1}}b=\widetilde{F}_{k}b_{1}\otimes b_{2},\quad\widetilde{E}_{k_{2}}b=b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{k}b_{2}.\end{split}

Also,
X^{\imath}
is identified with
X_{I^{\prime}}
in a way such that
\zeta_{k}:=\langle h_{k},\zeta\rangle=\langle h_{k_{2}}-h_{k_{1}},\zeta\rangle=\zeta_{k_{2}}
.

In Example
3.3.4
, we see that an
\imath
crystal can be thought of as a crystal over
\mathbf{U}_{I^{\prime}}
. Then, the
\mathbf{U}_{I^{\prime}}
-crystal structure of the
\imath
crystal
\mathcal{T}_{\zeta}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)
is described as follows: Let
k\in I^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{B}(\infty)\ni b=b_{1}\otimes b_{2}\in\mathcal{B}(-\infty)_{I^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)_{I^{\prime}}
.

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varphi_{k}(t_{\zeta}\otimes b)=\beta_{k_{2}}(t_{\zeta}\otimes b)=\max(\varphi_{k}(b_{2})+\zeta_{k}+\operatorname{wt}_{k}(b_{1}),\varphi_{k}(b_{1})),\\
&\varepsilon_{k}(t_{\zeta}\otimes b)=\beta_{k_{1}}(t_{\zeta}\otimes b)=\max(\varepsilon_{k}(b_{1})-\zeta_{k}-\operatorname{wt}_{k}(b_{2}),\varepsilon_{k}(b_{2})),\\
&\widetilde{F}_{k}(t_{\zeta}\otimes b)=\widetilde{B}_{k_{2}}(t_{\zeta}\otimes b)=\begin{cases}t_{\zeta}\otimes(b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{k}b_{2})&\text{ if }\varphi_{k}(b_{2})>\varepsilon_{k}(b_{1})-\zeta_{k},\\
t_{\zeta}\otimes(\widetilde{F}_{k}b_{1}\otimes b_{2})&\text{ if }\varphi_{k}(b_{2})\leq\varepsilon_{k}(b_{1})-\zeta_{k},\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{E}_{k}(t_{\zeta}\otimes b)=\widetilde{B}_{k_{1}}(t_{\zeta}\otimes b)=\begin{cases}t_{\zeta}\otimes(\widetilde{E}_{k}b_{1}\otimes b_{2})&\text{ if }\varepsilon_{k}(b_{1})>\varphi_{k}(b_{2})+\zeta_{k},\\
t_{\zeta}\otimes(b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{k}b_{2})&\text{ if }\varepsilon_{k}(b_{1})\leq\varphi_{k}(b_{2})+\zeta_{k}.\end{cases}\end{split}

On the other hand, the
\mathbf{U}_{I^{\prime}}
-crystal structure of
\mathcal{B}(-\infty)_{I^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{\zeta}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)_{I^{\prime}}
is described as follows: Let
k\in I^{\prime}
,
b_{1}\in\mathcal{B}(-\infty)_{I^{\prime}}
, and
b_{2}\in\mathcal{B}(\infty)_{I^{\prime}}
. First, we have

\varepsilon_{k}(b_{1}\otimes t_{\zeta})=\varepsilon_{k}(b_{1})-\zeta_{k},\quad\varphi_{k}(b_{1}\otimes t_{\zeta})=\varphi_{k}(b_{1}).

Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varphi_{k}(b_{1}\otimes t_{\zeta}\otimes b_{2})=\max(\varphi_{k}(b_{1}),\varphi_{k}(b_{2})+\zeta_{k}+\operatorname{wt}_{k}(b_{1})),\\
&\varepsilon_{k}(b_{1}\otimes t_{\zeta}\otimes b_{2})=\max(\varepsilon_{k}(b_{1})-\zeta_{k}-\operatorname{wt}_{k}(b_{2}),\varepsilon_{k}(b_{2})),\\
&\widetilde{F}_{k}(b_{1}\otimes t_{\zeta}\otimes b_{2})=\begin{cases}b_{1}\otimes t_{\zeta}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{k}b_{2}&\text{ if }\varepsilon_{k}(b_{1})-\zeta_{k}<\varphi_{k}(b_{2}),\\
\widetilde{F}_{k}b_{1}\otimes t_{\zeta}\otimes b_{2}&\text{ if }\varepsilon_{k}(b_{1})-\zeta_{k}\geq\varphi_{k}(b_{2}),\end{cases}\\
&\widetilde{E}_{k}(b_{1}\otimes t_{\zeta}\otimes b_{2})=\begin{cases}\widetilde{E}_{k}b_{1}\otimes t_{\zeta}\otimes b_{2}&\text{ if }\varepsilon_{k}(b_{1})-\zeta_{k}>\varphi_{k}(b_{2}),\\
b_{1}\otimes t_{\zeta}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{k}b_{2}&\text{ if }\varepsilon_{k}(b_{1})-\zeta_{k}\leq\varphi_{k}(b_{2}).\end{cases}\end{split}

Therefore, the
\imath
crystal
\mathcal{T}_{\zeta}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)
is essentially the same as the
\mathbf{U}_{I^{\prime}}
-crystal
\mathcal{B}(-\infty)_{I^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{\zeta}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)_{I^{\prime}}
.

6.2.
Stability of the
\imath
canonical bases

In this subsection, we lift the results obtained in the previous subsection to based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-modules. Lemmas
6.2.1
–
6.2.4
are preparations for this purpose.

Given a sequence
\mathbf{i}=(i_{1},\ldots,i_{r})\in I^{r}
, set

F_{\mathbf{i}}:=F_{i_{1}}\cdots F_{i_{r}},\quad B_{\mathbf{i}}:=B_{i_{1}}\cdots B_{i_{r}}.

We understand that
F_{\mathbf{i}}=1=B_{\mathbf{i}}
when
r=0
. Let
\mathcal{I}\subset\bigsqcup_{r\geq 0}I^{r}
be such that
\{F_{\mathbf{i}}\mid\mathbf{i}\in\mathcal{I}\}
forms a basis of
\mathbf{U}^{-}
. By
[
10
, Proposition 6.2]
, the set
\{B_{\mathbf{i}}K_{h}\mid\mathbf{i}\in\mathcal{I},\ h\in Y^{\imath}\}
forms a basis of
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
.

For each
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, set
\mathcal{I}_{r}:=\mathcal{I}\cap I^{r}
,
\mathcal{I}_{<r}:=\bigsqcup_{s<r}\mathcal{I}_{s}
. For each
\mathbf{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{r}
, set
|\mathbf{i}|:=r
. Then, for each
\mathbf{i}\in\mathcal{I}
, we have

(8)

\displaystyle\begin{split}B_{\mathbf{i}}-F_{\mathbf{i}}\in\sum_{\mathbf{i}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{<|\mathbf{i}|}}F_{\mathbf{i}^{\prime}}\mathbf{U}^{\geq 0},\end{split}

where
\mathbf{U}^{\geq 0}
denotes the subalgebra of
\mathbf{U}
generated by
K_{h},E_{i}
,
h\in Y
,
i\in I
.

Lemma 6.2.1
.

Let
M
be a weight
\mathbf{U}
-module,
\lambda\in X
, and
v\in M_{\lambda}
. Suppose that
E_{i}v=0
for all
i\in I
. Then, we have

\mathbf{U}^{\imath}v=\mathbf{U}v.

Proof.

The submodule
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}v
is spanned by vectors of the form
B_{\mathbf{i}}v
,
\mathbf{i}\in\mathcal{I}
. By equation (
8
), we have

B_{\mathbf{i}}v=F_{\mathbf{i}}v+\sum_{\mathbf{i}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{<|\mathbf{i}|}}c_{\mathbf{i}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}}F_{\mathbf{i}^{\prime}}v

for some
c_{\mathbf{i}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}}\in\mathbb{K}
. This implies that
F_{\mathbf{i}}v\in\mathbf{U}^{\imath}v
. Since
\{F_{\mathbf{i}}v\mid\mathbf{i}\in\mathcal{I}\}
spans
\mathbf{U}v
, the assertion follows.
∎

Lemma 6.2.2
.

Let
\lambda\in X
. Then, as a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module, we have

M(\lambda)\simeq\mathbf{U}^{\imath}/\sum_{h\in Y^{\imath}}\mathbf{U}^{\imath}(K_{h}-q^{\langle h,\lambda\rangle}).

Proof.

By Lemma
6.2.1
, we have
M(\lambda)=\mathbf{U}^{\imath}v_{\lambda}
. Hence, there exists a surjective
