\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module homomorphism
f:\mathbf{U}^{\imath}\rightarrow M(\lambda)
such that
f(1)=v_{\lambda}
. It is clear that
\sum_{h\in Y^{\imath}}\mathbf{U}^{\imath}(K_{h}-q^{\langle h,\lambda\rangle})\subset\operatorname{Ker}f
. Let us prove the opposite direction. Let
x\in\operatorname{Ker}f
. We can write
x=\sum_{(\mathbf{i},h)\in\mathcal{I}\times Y^{\imath}}c_{\mathbf{i},h}B_{\mathbf{i}}K_{h}
. Then, we have

(9)

\displaystyle\begin{split}0=xv_{\lambda}=\sum_{(\mathbf{i},h)}c_{\mathbf{i},h}q^{\langle h,\lambda\rangle}B_{\mathbf{i}}v_{\lambda}.\end{split}

On the other hand, by equation (
8
), we have

B_{\mathbf{i}}v_{\lambda}=F_{\mathbf{i}}v_{\lambda}+\sum_{\mathbf{i}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}_{<|\mathbf{i}|}}c_{\mathbf{i}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}}F_{\mathbf{i}^{\prime}}v_{\lambda}

for some
c_{\mathbf{i}^{\prime},\mathbf{i}}\in\mathbb{K}
. Since
\{F_{\mathbf{i}}v_{\lambda}\mid\mathbf{i}\in\mathcal{I}\}
forms a basis of
M(\lambda)
, we see that
\{B_{\mathbf{i}}v_{\lambda}\mid\mathbf{i}\in\mathcal{I}\}
forms a basis of
M(\lambda)
. This, together with identity (
9
), implies that

\sum_{h\in Y^{\imath}}c_{\mathbf{i},h}q^{\langle h,\lambda\rangle}=0\quad\text{ for all }\mathbf{i}\in\mathcal{I}.

Let
\mathbf{U}^{\imath,0}
denote the subalgebra of
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
generated by
K_{h}
,
h\in Y^{\imath}
, and, consider the algebra homomorphism
g:\mathbf{U}^{\imath,0}\rightarrow\mathbb{K}
which sends
K_{h}
to
q^{\langle h,\lambda\rangle}
. Then, we have
\sum_{h\in Y^{\imath}}c_{\mathbf{i},h}K_{h}\in\operatorname{Ker}g
. Since the subalgebra of
\mathbf{U}^{\imath,0}
generated by
K_{h}-q^{\langle h,\lambda\rangle}
,
h\in Y^{\imath}
is contained in the kernel of
g
, and the quotient algebra is one-dimensional, we see that the subalgebra coincides with the kernel of
g
. Therefore, we obtain

\sum_{h}c_{\mathbf{i},h}K_{h}\in\sum_{h^{\prime}\in Y^{\imath}}\mathbf{U}^{\imath,0}(K_{h^{\prime}}-q^{\langle h^{\prime},\lambda\rangle})\quad\text{ for all }\mathbf{i}\in\mathcal{I}.

Since
x=\sum_{\mathbf{i}\in\mathcal{I}}B_{\mathbf{i}}(\sum_{h\in Y^{\imath}}c_{\mathbf{i},h}K_{h})
, we conclude that

x\in\sum_{h\in Y^{\imath}}\mathbf{U}^{\imath}(K_{h}-q^{\langle h,\lambda\rangle}),

as desired. Thus, the proof completes.
∎

Lemma 6.2.3
.

Let
\lambda\in X
. Set
N(\lambda)
to be the
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-submodule of
M(\lambda)
generated by
b_{i}^{\langle h_{i},\lambda\rangle+1}v_{\lambda}
,
i\in I
, where for each
n\geq 0
, we set
b_{i}^{n+1}:=B_{i}^{n+1}
if
a_{i,\tau(i)}\neq 2
, and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&b_{i}^{n+1}:=\begin{cases}\prod_{l=0}^{n}(B_{i}-\operatorname{sgn}(s_{i})[|s_{i}|-n+2l]_{i})&\text{ if }n<|s_{i}|,\\
B_{i}\prod_{l=1}^{\frac{n-|s_{i}|}{2}}(B_{i}^{2}-[2l]_{i}^{2})&\\
\cdot\prod_{l=n-|s_{i}|+1}^{n}(B_{i}-\operatorname{sgn}(s_{i})[|s_{i}|-n+2l]_{i})&\text{ if }n\geq|s_{i}|\and\overline{n}=\overline{s_{i}},\\
\prod_{l=1}^{\frac{n-|s_{i}|+1}{2}}(B_{i}^{2}-[2l-1]_{i}^{2})&\\
\cdot\prod_{l=n-|s_{i}|+1}^{n}(B_{i}-\operatorname{sgn}(s_{i})[|s_{i}|-n+2l]_{i})&\text{ if }n\geq|s_{i}|\and\overline{n}\neq\overline{s_{i}}\end{cases}\end{split}

if
a_{i,\tau(i)}=2
. Then, we have
V(\lambda)=M(\lambda)/N(\lambda)
.

Proof.

Set
n_{i}:=\langle h_{i},\lambda\rangle
. Since

V(\lambda)=M(\lambda)/\sum_{i\in I}\mathbf{U}F_{i}^{n_{i}+1}v_{\lambda},

and
E_{i}F_{j}^{n_{j}+1}v_{\lambda}=0
for all
i,j\in I
, it suffices, by Lemma
6.2.1
, to show that
b_{i}^{n_{i}+1}v_{\lambda}=F_{i}^{n_{i}+1}v_{\lambda}
for all
i\in I
. Let us first consider the case when
a_{i,\tau(i)}\neq 2
. Since
B_{i}=F_{i}+q_{i}^{s_{i}}E_{\tau(i)}K_{i}^{-1}
and
E_{\tau(i)}F_{i}=F_{i}E_{\tau(i)}
, we have

B_{i}^{n_{i}+1}v_{\lambda}=F_{i}^{n_{i}+1}v_{\lambda},

as desired.

Next, let us consider the case when
a_{i,\tau(i)}=2
. From example
4.1.4
and the definitions of
\widetilde{B}_{i}
and
\beta_{i}
, we see that
B_{i}
acts on the
(n_{i}+1)
-dimensional irreducible
\mathbf{U}_{i}
-module diagonally with eigenvalues

•

\{\operatorname{sgn}(s_{i})[|s_{i}-n_{i}|+2l]_{i}\mid 0\leq l\leq n_{i}\}
when
n_{i}<|s_{i}|
,

•

\{0\}\sqcup\{\pm[2l]_{i}\mid 1\leq l\leq\frac{n_{i}-|s_{i}|}{2}\}\sqcup\{\operatorname{sgn}(s_{i})[|s_{i}|-n_{i}+2l]_{i}\mid n_{i}-|s_{i}|+1\leq l\leq n_{i}\}
when
n_{i}\geq|s_{i}|
and
\overline{n_{i}}=\overline{s_{i}}
,

•

\{\pm[2l-1]_{i}\mid 1\leq l\leq\frac{n_{i}-|s_{i}|+1}{2}\}\sqcup\{\operatorname{sgn}(s_{i})[|s_{i}|-n_{i}+2l]_{i}\mid n_{i}-|s_{i}|+1\leq l\leq n_{i}\}
when
n_{i}\geq|s_{i}|
and
\overline{n_{i}}\neq\overline{s_{i}}
.

This implies that

(10)

\displaystyle\begin{split}b_{i}^{n_{i}+1}v_{\lambda}=0\quad\text{ in }V(\lambda).\end{split}

On the other hand,
b_{i}
is of the form

b_{i}^{n_{i}+1}=B_{i}^{n_{i}+1}+\sum_{k=0}^{n_{i}}c_{k}B_{i}^{k}

for some
c_{k}\in\mathbb{K}
. Then, equation (
8
) implies that

b_{i}^{n_{i}+1}v_{\lambda}=F_{i}^{n_{i}+1}v_{\lambda}+\sum_{k=0}^{n_{i}}c^{\prime}_{k}F_{i}^{k}v_{\lambda}

for some
c^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathbb{K}
, and hence,
b_{i}^{n_{i}+1}v_{\lambda}=\sum_{k=0}^{n_{i}}c^{\prime}_{k}F_{i}^{k}v_{\lambda}
in
V(\lambda)
. Since
\{F_{i}^{k}v_{\lambda}\mid 0\leq k\leq n_{i}\}
forms a linearly independent set of
V(\lambda)
, identity (
10
) implies that
c^{\prime}_{k}=0
for all
k
. Therefore, we obtain

b_{i}^{n_{i}+1}v_{\lambda}=F_{i}^{n_{i}+1}v_{\lambda},

as desired. Thus, the proof completes.
∎

Let us recall that we have fixed
\sigma\in X^{+}
at the beginning of Subsection
6.1
.

Lemma 6.2.4
.

Let
\nu\in X^{+}
and
b\in\mathcal{B}(\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu))
. Then, we have

\beta_{i}(b)=0,\quad\widetilde{B}_{i}b=0\quad\text{ for all }i\in I

if and only if
b=b_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)}
.

Proof.

By Corollary
5.2.2
, we see that
\beta_{i}(b)=0
and
\widetilde{B}_{i}b=0
if and only if

•

|s_{i}|\leq\varphi
,
\overline{s_{i}}=\overline{\varphi_{i}(b)}
, and
\varepsilon_{i}(b)=0
when
a_{i,\tau(i)}=2
,

•

\varphi_{i}(b)\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b)+s_{i}
and
\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}(b)=0
when
a_{i,\tau(i)}\neq 2
.

It is easily verified that
b_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)}
satisfies the latter condition. Conversely, if
b
satisfies the latter condition, we have
\varepsilon_{i}(b)=0
for all
i\in I
. This implies that
b=b_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)}
. Thus, the proof completes.
∎

Now, recall from Proposition
3.2.6
(see also Example
3.2.4
) that
V(\lambda)^{\sigma}
is a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module for all
\lambda\in X^{+}
.

Proposition 6.2.5
.

Let
\nu\in X^{+}
. Then, there exists a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module homomorphism
\gamma_{\nu}:V(\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu))\rightarrow V(0)^{\sigma}
such that
\gamma_{\nu}(v_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)})=v_{0}^{\sigma}
.

Proof.

Recall that
V(0)^{\sigma}
is isomorphic to the quotient of
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
factored by the left
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-submodule generated by
B_{i}
,
i\in I
and
K_{h}-q^{\langle h,\sigma\rangle}
,
h\in Y^{\imath}
. On the other hand, by Lemmas
6.2.2
and
6.2.3
,
V(\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu))
is isomorphic to the quotient of
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
factored by the left
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-submodule generated by
b_{i}^{\langle h_{i},\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)\rangle+1}
,
i\in I
and
K_{h}-q^{\langle h,\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)\rangle}
,
h\in Y^{\imath}
. Noting that
b_{i}^{\langle h_{i},\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)\rangle+1}\in\mathbf{U}^{\imath}B_{i}
and
\langle h,\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)\rangle=\langle h,\sigma\rangle
for all
i\in I
and
h\in Y^{\imath}
, we see that there exists a surjective
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module homomorphism
\gamma_{\nu}:V(\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu))\rightarrow V(0)^{\sigma}
such that
\gamma_{\nu}(v_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)})=v_{0}^{\sigma}
.

Let us show that
\gamma_{\nu}
is a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module homomorphism. Set
K:=\operatorname{Ker}\gamma_{\nu}
. Since
\wp^{*}
preserves
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
, the complement
K^{\perp}\subset V(\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu))
of
K
is isomorphic to
V(0)^{\sigma}
. Hence, there exists
v^{\prime}_{0}\in V(\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu))
such that
K^{\perp}=\mathbb{K}v^{\prime}_{0}
. We may assume that
v^{\prime}_{0}\in\mathcal{L}(\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu))
and
b^{\prime}_{0}:=\mathrm{ev}_{\infty}(v^{\prime}_{0})\neq 0
. Since
K^{\perp}\simeq V(0)^{\sigma}
, we must have

\beta_{i}(v^{\prime}_{0})=0,\ \widetilde{B}_{i}v^{\prime}_{0}=0\quad\text{ for all }i\in I.

Since
(\mathcal{L}(\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)),\mathcal{B}(\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)))
is an
\imath
crystal base,
b^{\prime}_{0}
is a linear combination of vectors
b\in\mathcal{B}(\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu))
such that
\beta_{i}(b)=0
for all
i\in I
(cf. Definition
4.0.1
).
Also, by Theorem
5.2.3
and Example
3.3.10
(
3
), (
5
), and (
6
), we see that if
b\in\mathcal{B}(\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu))
satisfies
\beta_{i}(b)=0
for all
i\in I
, then we have
\widetilde{B}_{i}b=0
for all
i\in I
.
Therefore, the element
b^{\prime}_{0}
is a linear combination of elements
b\in\mathcal{B}(\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu))
with
\beta_{i}(b)=0
and
\widetilde{B}_{i}b=0
for all
i\in I
. By Lemma
6.2.4
, this implies that

b^{\prime}_{0}=b_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)}.

Let
b\in\mathcal{B}(\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu))
. Then, we have

(G^{\imath}(b),v^{\prime}_{0})\equiv(b,b^{\prime}_{0})=(b,b_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)})=\delta_{b,b_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)}}\quad\pmod{q^{-1}\mathbb{K}_{\infty}}.

Hence, we see that

\gamma_{\nu}(G^{\imath}(b))=\gamma_{\nu}(\frac{(G^{\imath}(b),v^{\prime}_{0})}{(v^{\prime}_{0},v^{\prime}_{0})}v^{\prime}_{0})=\frac{(G^{\imath}(b),v^{\prime}_{0})}{(v^{\prime}_{0},v^{\prime}_{0})}v_{0}^{\sigma}\in(\delta_{b,b_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)}}+q^{-1}\mathbb{K}_{\infty})v_{0}^{\sigma}.

On the other hand, since
G^{\imath}(b)\in V(\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu))_{\mathbf{A}}=\dot{\mathbf{U}}^{\imath}_{\mathbf{A}}v_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)}
, we have
\gamma_{\nu}(G^{\imath}(b))\in\dot{\mathbf{U}}^{\imath}_{\mathbf{A}}v_{0}^{\sigma}=V(0)^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{A}}
. Similarly, since
G^{\imath}(b)
is bar-invariant, so is
\gamma_{\nu}(G^{\imath}(b))
. Thus, we obtain

\gamma_{\nu}(G^{\imath}(b))=0

if
b\neq b_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu)}
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proposition 6.2.6
.

Let
\lambda\in X^{+}
. Then, there exists a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module homomorphism
\rho_{\lambda}:V(\sigma+\lambda)\rightarrow V(\lambda)^{\sigma}
such that
\rho_{\lambda}(G^{\imath}(\pi_{\sigma+\lambda}(b)))=G^{\imath}(\pi_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma})
for all
b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty)
.

Proof.

Let
\eta_{\sigma,\lambda}:V(\sigma+\lambda)\rightarrow V(\sigma)\otimes V(\lambda)
denote the
\mathbf{U}
-module homomorphism such that
\eta(v_{\sigma+\lambda})=v_{\sigma}\otimes v_{\lambda}
. By
[
14
, Proposition 25.1.2]
, we have

\eta_{\sigma,\lambda}(G(\pi_{\sigma+\lambda}(b)))\in v_{\sigma}\otimes G(\pi_{\lambda}(b))+q^{-1}\mathcal{L}(\sigma)\otimes\mathcal{L}(\lambda)\quad\text{ for all }b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty;\lambda),

and

(\eta_{\sigma,\lambda}(G(\pi_{\sigma+\lambda}(b))),v_{\sigma}\otimes G(b^{\prime}))\in q^{-1}\mathbb{K}_{\infty}\quad\text{ for all }b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty){\setminus}\mathcal{B}(\infty;\lambda),\ b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}(\lambda).

Composing
\gamma_{0}
in Proposition
6.2.5
on the first factor, we obtain a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module homomorphism

\rho_{\lambda}:=(\gamma_{0}\otimes\operatorname{id})\circ\eta_{\sigma,\lambda}:V(\sigma+\lambda)\rightarrow V(\lambda)^{\sigma}.

Then, we see that
\rho_{\lambda}(G^{\imath}(b))
is
\imath
bar-invariant, and belongs to the intersection of the
\mathbb{K}_{\infty}
-form and the
\mathbf{A}
-form. Moreover, for each
b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty)
, we have

\mathrm{ev}_{\infty}(\rho_{\lambda}(G^{\imath}(\pi_{\sigma+\lambda}(b))))=\pi_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma}.

By above, we conclude that

\rho_{\lambda}(G^{\imath}(\pi_{\sigma+\lambda}(b)))=G^{\imath}(\pi_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma})

as desired. Thus, the proof completes.
∎

Proposition 6.2.7
.

Let
\lambda,\nu\in X^{+}
. Then, there exists a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module homomorphism
\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}:V(\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu))^{\sigma}\rightarrow V(\lambda)^{\sigma}
such that

\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}(G^{\imath}(\pi_{\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(b)^{\sigma}))=G^{\imath}(\pi_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma})

for all
b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty)
.

Proof.

Consider the linear map
\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}:V(\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu))^{\sigma}\rightarrow V(\lambda)^{\sigma}
given by

\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}(G^{\imath}(\pi_{\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(b)^{\sigma}))=G^{\imath}(\pi_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma}),\quad b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty).

In order to prove the assertion, it suffices to show that
\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}
is a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module homomorphism. Note that the following diagram

\textstyle{V(\sigma+\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu),\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}}
\textstyle{V(\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu))\otimes V(\lambda)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\gamma_{\nu}}\otimes\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{V(\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu))^{\sigma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}}
\textstyle{V(\lambda)^{\sigma}}

commutes. For each
b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty)
and
x\in\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}(x\cdot G^{\imath}(\pi_{\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(b)^{\sigma}))&=\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}(x\cdot\rho_{\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(G^{\imath}(\pi_{\sigma+\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(b))))\\
&=(\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}\circ\rho_{\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)})(x\cdot G^{\imath}(\pi_{\sigma+\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(b)))\\
&=((\gamma_{\nu}\otimes\operatorname{id})\circ\eta_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu),\lambda})(xG^{\imath}(\pi_{\sigma+\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(b)))\\
&=x((\gamma_{\nu}\otimes\operatorname{id})\circ\eta_{\sigma+\nu+\tau(\nu),\lambda})(G^{\imath}(\pi_{\sigma+\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(b)))\\
&=x(\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}\circ\rho_{\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)})(G^{\imath}(\pi_{\sigma+\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(b)))\\
&=x\cdot G^{\imath}(\pi_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma})\\
&=x\cdot\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}(G^{\imath}(\pi_{\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(b)^{\sigma})).\end{split}

This shows that
\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}
is a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module homomorphism. Hence, the proof completes.
∎

Now, for each
\zeta\in X^{\imath}
, we obtain a projective system
\{V(\lambda)^{\sigma}\}_{\lambda\in X^{+},\overline{\lambda}=\zeta}
of based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-modules and based homomorphisms
\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}
.

Theorem 6.2.8
.

Let
\zeta\in X^{\imath}
. Then, for each
\lambda\in X^{+}
such that
\overline{\sigma+\lambda}=\zeta
, there exists a based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module homomorphism
\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda}:\dot{\mathbf{U}}^{\imath}\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}\rightarrow V(\lambda)^{\sigma}
such that

\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda}(G^{\imath}_{\zeta}(b))=G^{\imath}(\pi_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma})

for all
b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty)
. Moreover,
\dot{\mathbf{U}}^{\imath}\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}
is the projective limit of
\{V(\lambda)^{\sigma}\}_{\lambda\in X^{+},\overline{\sigma+\lambda}=\zeta}
in the category of based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-modules and based homomorphisms.

Proof.

Let
\lambda\in X^{+}
be such that
\overline{\sigma+\lambda}=\zeta
. There exists a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module homomorphism
\dot{\mathbf{U}}^{\imath}\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}\rightarrow V(\sigma+\lambda)
which sends
\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}
to
v_{\sigma+\lambda}
. Combining
\rho_{\lambda}
, we obtain a
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module homomorphism
\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda}:\dot{\mathbf{U}}^{\imath}\rightarrow V(\lambda)^{\sigma}
which sends
\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}
to
v_{\lambda}^{\sigma}
. Then, for each
\nu\in X^{+}
, we have

\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}\circ\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}=\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda}.

Let us show that
\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda}
is based. Let
b\in\mathcal{B}(\infty)
. By Theorem
3.2.7
there exists
\nu\in X^{+}
such that

G^{\imath}_{\zeta}(b)v_{\sigma+\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}=G^{\imath}(\pi_{\sigma+\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(b)).

Then, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda}(G^{\imath}_{\zeta}(b))&=\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}\circ\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(G^{\imath}_{\zeta}(b))\\
&=\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}\circ\rho_{\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(G^{\imath}(\pi_{\sigma+\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(b)))\\
&=\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda,\nu}(G^{\imath}(\pi_{\lambda+\nu+\tau(\nu)}(b)^{\sigma}))\\
&=G^{\imath}(\pi_{\lambda}(b)^{\sigma}),\end{split}

as desired.

The universality can be proved in a similar way to Theorem
6.1.8
. Thus, the proof completes.
∎

Remark 6.2.9
.

The previous theorem partly settles Bao and Wang’s conjecture in
[
2
, Remark 6.18]
.
Actually, when
s_{i}=0
for all
i\in I_{\tau}
, we can take
\sigma=0
, and in this case, the previous theorem states that the
\imath
canonical bases are stable (or strongly compatible in Bao-Wang’s terminology).

The very strict
\imath
crystal morphism
\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda}:\mathcal{T}_{\zeta}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\lambda)^{\sigma}
can be thought of as the crystal limit of the based
\mathbf{U}^{\imath}
-module homomoprhism
\pi^{\imath}_{\lambda}:\dot{\mathbf{U}}^{\imath}\mathbf{1}_{\zeta}\rightarrow V(\lambda)^{\sigma}
. Hence, it is reasonable to denote
\bigsqcup_{\zeta\in X^{\imath}}\mathcal{T}_{\zeta}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)
by
\dot{\mathcal{B}}^{\imath}
, and call it the
\imath
crystal basis of
\dot{\mathbf{U}}^{\imath}
.

Example 6.2.10
.

Suppose that our Satake diagram is of diagonal type. As we have seen in Example
6.1.9
, the
\imath
crystal
\mathcal{T}_{\zeta}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)
is essentially the same as the
\mathbf{U}_{I^{\prime}}
-crystal
\mathcal{B}(-\infty)_{I^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{\zeta}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)_{I^{\prime}}
. Therefore, our description
\dot{\mathcal{B}}^{\imath}=\bigsqcup_{\zeta\in X^{\imath}}\mathcal{T}_{\zeta}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)
of the
\imath
crystal basis of the modified
\imath
quantum group
\dot{\mathbf{U}}^{\imath}
is essentially the same as Kashiwara’s description
[
9
, Theorem 3.1.1]

\dot{\mathcal{B}}=\bigsqcup_{\zeta\in X_{I^{\prime}}}\mathcal{B}(-\infty)_{I^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{\zeta}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\infty)_{I^{\prime}}
of the crystal basis of the modified quantum group
\dot{\mathbf{U}}_{I^{\prime}}
.

7.
Proofs

In this section, we give proofs of Propositions
5.1.1
and
5.1.3
, and complete our argument.

7.1.
Proof of Proposition
5.1.1

Let
b_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{1}
,
b_{2}\in\mathcal{B}_{2}
, and
i\in I
. Set
b:=b_{1}\otimes b_{2}
,
\beta_{i}:=\beta_{i}(b_{1})
,
\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}:=\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}(b_{1})
,
\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}:=\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b_{1})
,
\varepsilon_{i}:=\varepsilon_{i}(b_{2})
,
\varphi_{i}:=\varphi_{i}(b_{2})
,
\operatorname{wt}:=\operatorname{wt}(b_{2})
,
\operatorname{wt}_{i}:=\operatorname{wt}_{i}(b_{2})
.

Lemma 7.1.1
.

Suppose that
a_{i,\tau(i)}=-1
. Then, the following hold:

(1)

If
B_{i}(b)=\beta_{\tau(i)}
, then

\beta_{\tau(i)}(b)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b)-s_{i}=\max(E_{i}(b)-1,B_{i}(b),F_{i}(b))+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}.

(2)

If
B_{i}(b)\neq\beta_{\tau(i)}
, then

\beta_{\tau(i)}(b)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b)-s_{i}=\max(E_{i}(b)-1,B_{i}(b)-1,F_{i}(b))+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}.

Proof.

The assertions follow from definitions and direct calculation.
∎

First, we confirm Definition
3.3.2
(
2
). Let
b^{\prime}=b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{B}
be such that
(\widetilde{B}_{i}b,b^{\prime})\neq 0
. By the definition of
\widetilde{B}_{i}b
, we see that
b^{\prime}
is either
b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2}
,
b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2}
, or
b^{\prime\prime}_{1}\otimes b_{2}
for some
b^{\prime\prime}_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{1}
with
(\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1},b^{\prime\prime}_{1})\neq 0
. Each element has weight
\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}(b)-\overline{\alpha_{i}}
. This confirms the axiom.

Below, we confirm the remaining axioms

7.1.1.
When
a_{i,\tau(i)}=2

By the definition of
\beta_{i}(b)
, we have
\beta_{i}(b)\notin\mathbb{Z}
if and only if
\beta_{i},\varphi_{i}\notin\mathbb{Z}
. In this case, we have
\beta_{i}(b)=\beta_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{i}\in\{-\infty_{\mathrm{ev}},-\infty_{\mathrm{odd}}\}
and

\widetilde{B}_{i}b=\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\otimes b_{2}=0.

Hence, Definition
3.3.2
(
1
) and (
5a
) are satisfied.

(1)

When
\beta_{i}\leq\varphi_{i}
and
\overline{\beta_{i}}\neq\overline{\varphi_{i}}
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b)=\varepsilon_{i}+1,\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}b=b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2}.\end{split}

Noting that
\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}=\overline{\beta_{i}+s_{i}}
and
\operatorname{wt}_{i}=\varphi_{i}-\varepsilon_{i}
, we see that

\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b)=\overline{\beta_{i}+s_{i}+\operatorname{wt}_{i}}=\overline{s_{i}-\varepsilon_{i}+1}=\overline{\beta_{i}(b)+s_{i}}.

This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
5b
).

Let
b^{\prime}=b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{B}
be such that
(\widetilde{B}_{i}b,b^{\prime})\neq 0
. Then, we have
b^{\prime}=b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2}=\widetilde{B}_{i}b
. Since
\varphi_{i}(b^{\prime}_{2})=\varphi_{i}-1\geq\beta_{i}
(note that
\beta_{i}\leq\varphi_{i}
and
\overline{\beta_{i}}\neq\overline{\varphi_{i}}
implies
\beta_{i}<\varphi_{i}
) and
\overline{\varphi_{i}(b^{\prime}_{2})}\neq\overline{\varphi_{i}}\neq\overline{\beta_{i}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b^{\prime})=\varepsilon_{i}(b^{\prime}_{2})=\varepsilon_{i}+1=\beta_{i}(b),\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}b^{\prime}=b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{i}b^{\prime}_{2}=b_{1}\otimes b_{2}=b.\end{split}

This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
3
), (
4
), and (
5c
).

(2)

When
\beta_{i}>\varphi_{i}
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b)=\beta_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{i},\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}b=\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\otimes b_{2}.\end{split}

Since
\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}=\overline{\beta_{i}+s_{i}}
, we obtain

\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b)=\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}+\overline{\operatorname{wt}_{i}}=\overline{\beta_{i}+s_{i}+\operatorname{wt}_{i}}=\overline{\beta_{i}(b)+s_{i}}.

This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
5b
).

Let
b^{\prime}=b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{B}
be such that
(\widetilde{B}_{i}b,b^{\prime})\neq 0
. Then, we have
(\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1},b^{\prime}_{1})\neq 0
, and
b^{\prime}_{2}=b_{2}
. Since
\beta_{i}(b^{\prime}_{1})=\beta_{i}
, we obtain
\beta_{i}(b^{\prime}_{1})>\varphi_{i}
. Hence, it follows that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b^{\prime})=\beta_{i}(b^{\prime}_{1})-\operatorname{wt}_{i}(b^{\prime}_{2})=\beta_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{i}=\beta_{i}(b),\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}b^{\prime}=\widetilde{B}_{i}b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b^{\prime}_{2}=\widetilde{B}_{i}b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b_{2},\\
&(b,\widetilde{B}_{i}b^{\prime})=(b_{1},\widetilde{B}_{i}b^{\prime}_{1})=(\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1},b^{\prime}_{1})=(\widetilde{B}_{i}b,b^{\prime}).\end{split}

This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
3
) and (
5c
). Furthermore, if
\widetilde{B}_{i}b\in\mathcal{B}
, we have
b^{\prime}_{1}=\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}
, and hence,

\widetilde{B}_{i}b^{\prime}=\widetilde{B}_{i}b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b_{2}=b_{1}\otimes b_{2}=b.

This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
4
).

(3)

When
\beta_{i}\leq\varphi_{i}
and
\overline{\beta_{i}}=\overline{\varphi_{i}}
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b)=\varepsilon_{i},\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}b=b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{i}b_{2}.\end{split}

Noting that
\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}=\overline{\beta_{i}+s_{i}}
and
\operatorname{wt}_{i}=\varphi_{i}-\varepsilon_{i}
, we see that

\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b)=\overline{\beta_{i}+s_{i}+\operatorname{wt}_{i}}=\overline{s_{i}-\varepsilon_{i}}=\overline{\beta_{i}(b)+s_{i}}.

This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
5b
).

Let
b^{\prime}=b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{B}
be such that
(\widetilde{B}_{i}b,b^{\prime})\neq 0
. Then, we have
b^{\prime}=b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{i}b_{2}=\widetilde{B}_{i}b
. Since
\varphi_{i}(b^{\prime}_{2})=\varphi_{i}+1>\beta_{i}
and
\overline{\varphi_{i}(b^{\prime}_{2})}\neq\overline{\varphi_{i}}=\overline{\beta_{i}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b^{\prime})=\varepsilon_{i}(b^{\prime}_{2})+1=\varepsilon_{i}=\beta_{i}(b),\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}b^{\prime}=b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b^{\prime}_{2}=b_{1}\otimes b_{2}=b.\end{split}

This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
3
), (
4
), and (
5c
).

7.1.2.
When
a_{i,\tau(i)}=0

By the definition of
\beta_{i}(b)
, we have
\beta_{i}(b)\notin\mathbb{Z}
if and only if
\varphi_{i},\beta_{i},\varphi_{\tau(i)}=-\infty
. In this case, we have
\beta_{i}(b)=\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}=-\infty
and

\widetilde{B}_{i}b=b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2}=0.

Hence, Definition
3.3.2
(
1
) and (
6a
) are satisfied.

Also, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\beta_{\tau(i)}(b)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b)&=\max(\varphi_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{\tau(i)}-\operatorname{wt}_{i},\beta_{\tau(i)}-\operatorname{wt}_{i},\varepsilon_{i})+(\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}+\operatorname{wt}_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)})\\
&=\max(\varepsilon_{\tau(i)},\beta_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)},\varphi_{i}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)})=\beta_{i}(b).\end{split}

This confirms
3.3.2
(
6b
).

(1)

When
\varphi_{i}>\beta_{\tau(i)},\varphi_{\tau(i)}-\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b)=\varphi_{i}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)},\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}b=b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2}.\end{split}

Let
b^{\prime}=b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{B}
be such that
(\widetilde{B}_{i}b,b^{\prime})\neq 0
. Then, we have
b^{\prime}=b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2}=\widetilde{B}_{i}b
. We compute as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{2})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}<\varphi_{i}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i},\\
&\beta_{i}(b^{\prime}_{1})=\beta_{i}=\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}<\varphi_{i}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i},\\
&\varphi_{i}(b^{\prime}_{2})-\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{1})=(\varphi_{i}-1)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}.\end{split}

This implies that
F_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}),B_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})\leq E_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})
, and hence,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})=\varepsilon_{i}(b^{\prime}_{2})=\varepsilon_{i}+1,\\
&\beta_{i}(b^{\prime})=\beta_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b^{\prime})=(\varepsilon_{i}+1)+(\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}+\operatorname{wt}_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}-2)=\beta_{i}(b)-1,\\
&\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}=b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{i}b^{\prime}_{2}=b_{1}\otimes b_{2}=b.\end{split}

This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
3
), (
4
), and (
6c
).

(2)

When
\varphi_{i}\leq\beta_{\tau(i)}>\varphi_{\tau(i)}-\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b)=\beta_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)},\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}b=\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\otimes b_{2}.\end{split}

Let
b^{\prime}=b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{B}
be such that
(\widetilde{B}_{i}b,b^{\prime})\neq 0
. Then, we have
b^{\prime}=\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\otimes b_{2}=\widetilde{B}_{i}b
. We compute as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{2})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}<\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}=\beta_{i},\\
&\beta_{i}(b^{\prime}_{1})=\beta_{i}-1,\\
&\varphi_{i}(b^{\prime}_{2})-\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{1})=\varphi_{i}+(\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-2)\leq\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-2=\beta_{i}-2.\end{split}

This implies that
F_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})\leq B_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})>E_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})
, and hence,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})=\beta_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{1})-\operatorname{wt}_{i}(b^{\prime}_{2})=(\beta_{\tau(i)}+1)-\operatorname{wt}_{i},\\
&\beta_{i}(b^{\prime})=\beta_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b^{\prime})=(\beta_{\tau(i)}-\operatorname{wt}_{i}+1)+(\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}+\operatorname{wt}_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}-2)=\beta_{i}(b)-1,\\
&\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}=\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b^{\prime}_{2}=b_{1}\otimes b_{2}=b.\end{split}

This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
3
), (
4
), and (
6c
).

(3)

When
\varphi_{i},\beta_{\tau(i)}\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)}-\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b)=\varepsilon_{\tau(i)},\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}b=b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2}.\end{split}

Let
b^{\prime}=b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{B}
be such that
(\widetilde{B}_{i}b,b^{\prime})\neq 0
. Then, we have
b^{\prime}=b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2}=\widetilde{B}_{i}b
. We compute as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{2})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}+1,\\
&\beta_{i}(b^{\prime}_{1})=\beta_{i}=\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)},\\
&\varphi_{i}(b^{\prime}_{2})-\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{1})=\varphi_{i}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)}.\end{split}

This implies that
F_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})>B_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}),E_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})
, and hence,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{2})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{1})-\operatorname{wt}_{i}(b^{\prime}_{2})=(\varphi_{\tau(i)}+1)-\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{i},\\
&\beta_{i}(b^{\prime})=\beta_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b^{\prime})=(\varphi_{\tau(i)}-\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{i}+1)+(\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}+\operatorname{wt}_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}-2)=\beta_{i}(b)-1,\\
&\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}=b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}_{2}=b_{1}\otimes b_{2}=b.\end{split}

This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
3
), (
4
), and (
6c
).

7.1.3.
When
a_{i,\tau(i)}=-1

By the definition of
\beta_{i}(b)
, we have
\beta_{i}(b)\notin\mathbb{Z}
if and only if
\varphi_{i},\beta_{i},\varphi_{\tau(i)}=-\infty
. In this case, we have
\beta_{i}(b)=\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}=-\infty
, and

\widetilde{B}_{i}b=b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2}=0.

Hence, Definition
3.3.2
(
1
) and (
7a
) are satisfied.

(1)

When
B_{i}(b)<F_{i}(b)=E_{i}(b)+1
and
\varphi_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}+1
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b)=F_{i}(b)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}=(\varphi_{\tau(i)}+1)-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}=\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}+1,\\
&\beta_{\tau(i)}(b)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b)-s_{i}=F_{i}(b)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}=\beta_{i}(b),\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}b=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2}\notin\mathcal{B}.\end{split}

For the second line of the identity above, we used Lemma
7.1.1
. This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
4
), (
7b
), and (
7c
).

Let
b^{\prime}=b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{B}
be such that
(\widetilde{B}_{i}b,b^{\prime})\neq 0
. Then, we have
b^{\prime}=b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2}
. By our assumption, we see that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F_{i}(b^{\prime})=\varphi_{i}-1<F_{i}(b),\\
&B_{i}(b^{\prime})=B_{i}(b)<F_{i}(b),\\
&E_{i}(b^{\prime})=E_{i}(b)+1=F_{i}(b),\end{split}

and hence,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b^{\prime})=E_{i}(b^{\prime})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})=\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{2})=\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}=\beta_{i}(b)-1,\\
&\beta_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b^{\prime})-s_{i}=(E_{i}(b^{\prime})-1)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{2})\neq\beta_{i}(b^{\prime}).\end{split}

For the last line of the identity above, we used Lemma
7.1.1
. This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
7d
).

Now, we compute as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}+1,\\
&B_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})=\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}+1\leq\beta_{i}+1<\varphi_{\tau(i)}+2,\\
&E_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})=(\varphi_{i}-1)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}+1=\varphi_{i}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}=\varphi_{\tau(i)}+1,\\
&\beta_{i}(b^{\prime}_{1})=\beta_{i}<\varphi_{\tau(i)}+1,\\
&\widetilde{E}_{i}b^{\prime}_{2}=b_{2}\neq 0,\\
&\varphi_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{E}_{i}b^{\prime}_{2})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{2})-1.\end{split}

This implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{i}b^{\prime}_{2}+b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}_{2})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(b+b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}_{2}),\end{split}

which shows that

(\widetilde{B}_{i}b,b^{\prime})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}=(b,\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}).

This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
3
).

(2)

When
F_{i}(b)\leq B_{i}(b)=E_{i}(b)+1
and
\beta_{i}(\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1})=\beta_{i}-2
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{\tau(i)}=B_{i}(b),\\
&\beta_{i}(b)=B_{i}(b)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}=(\varphi_{\tau(i)}+1)-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}=\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}+1,\\
&\beta_{\tau(i)}(b)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b)-s_{i}=B_{i}(b)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}=\beta_{i}(b),\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}b=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\otimes b_{2}\notin\mathcal{B}.\end{split}

This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
4
), (
7b
), and (
7c
).

Let
b^{\prime}=b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{B}
be such that
(\widetilde{B}_{i}b,b^{\prime})\neq 0
. Then, we have
b^{\prime}=\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\otimes b_{2}
. By our assumption, we see that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F_{i}(b^{\prime})=\varphi_{i}\leq B_{i}(b),\\
&B_{i}(b^{\prime})=(\beta_{i}-2)-(\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-3)+s_{i}=B_{i}(b)+1,\\
&E_{i}(b^{\prime})=E_{i}(b)+3=B_{i}(b)+2,\end{split}

and hence,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b^{\prime})=E_{i}(b^{\prime})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b^{\prime}_{1})-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}=\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{2})=\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}=\beta_{i}(b)-1,\\
&\beta_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b^{\prime})-s_{i}=(E_{i}(b^{\prime})-1)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b^{\prime}_{1})-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}\neq\beta_{i}(b^{\prime}).\end{split}

This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
7d
).

Now, we compute as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})=\varphi_{\tau(i)},\\
&B_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})=\beta_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{1})+(\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-3)-s_{i}+1=\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-1=\varphi_{\tau(i)},\\
&E_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})=\varphi_{i}+(\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-3)-s_{i}+1=\varphi_{i}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-2\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)}-1,\\
&\beta_{i}(b^{\prime}_{1})=\beta_{i}-2=\varphi_{\tau(i)}-1.\end{split}

This implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b^{\prime}_{2}+b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}_{2})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(b+b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{\tau(i)}b_{2}),\end{split}

which shows that

(\widetilde{B}_{i}b,b^{\prime})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}=(b,\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}).

This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
3
).

(3)

When
E_{i}(b)<F_{i}(b)=B_{i}(b)\neq\beta_{\tau(i)}
. Since
B_{i}(b)\neq\beta_{\tau(i)}
, we have

\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{1},\quad\beta_{i}(\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1})=\beta_{i}-1,\quad\beta_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1})=\beta_{\tau(i)}+2

if
\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\neq 0
. Also, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b)=B_{i}(b)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}=\beta_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)},\\
&\beta_{\tau(i)}(b)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b)-s_{i}=F_{i}(b)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}=\beta_{i}(b),\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}b=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\otimes b_{2}+b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2})\notin\mathcal{B}.\end{split}

This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
4
), (
7b
), and (
7c
).

Let
b^{\prime}=b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{B}
be such that
(\widetilde{B}_{i}b,b^{\prime})\neq 0
. Then, we have
b^{\prime}=\widetilde{B}_{i}b_{1}\otimes b_{2}
or
b^{\prime}=b^{\prime\prime}=b^{\prime\prime}_{1}\otimes b^{\prime\prime}_{2}=b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{F}_{i}b_{2}
. By our assumption, we see that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F_{i}(b^{\prime})=\varphi_{i}=B_{i}(b),\\
&B_{i}(b^{\prime})=(\beta_{i}-1)-(\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-3)+s_{i}=B_{i}(b)+2,\\
&E_{i}(b^{\prime})=E_{i}(b)+3\leq B_{i}(b)+2,\end{split}

and hence,

(11)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b^{\prime})=B_{i}(b^{\prime})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b^{\prime}_{1})-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}=\beta_{i}(b^{\prime}_{1})-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}=\beta_{i}(b)-1,\\
&\beta_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b^{\prime})-s_{i}=(B_{i}(b^{\prime})-1)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b^{\prime}_{1})-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}\neq\beta_{i}(b^{\prime}).\end{split}

Also, we compute as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F_{i}(b^{\prime\prime})=\varphi_{i}-1=B_{i}(b)-1,\\
&B_{i}(b^{\prime\prime})=B_{i}(b),\\
&E_{i}(b^{\prime\prime})\leq E_{i}(b)+1\leq B_{i}(b).\end{split}

This shows that

(12)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b^{\prime\prime})=B_{i}(b^{\prime\prime})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime\prime}_{2})=\beta_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime\prime}_{2})=\beta_{i}(b)-1,\\
&\beta_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime\prime})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b^{\prime\prime})-s_{i}=(B_{i}(b^{\prime\prime})-1)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime\prime}_{2})\neq\beta_{i}(b^{\prime\prime}).\end{split}

Identities (
11
) and (
12
) confirm Definition
3.3.2
(
7d
).

Now, we compute as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}<\beta_{i},\\
&B_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})=(\beta_{\tau(i)}+2)+(\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-3)-s_{i}+1=\beta_{i}-1,\\
&E_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})=\varphi_{i}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-2=\beta_{i}-2,\\
&\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}_{1}=b_{1}\in\mathcal{B}_{1},\\
&\beta_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}_{1})=\beta_{\tau(i)}=\beta_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{1})-2.\end{split}

This implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b^{\prime}_{2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}b.\end{split}

The last identity shows that

(13)

\displaystyle(\widetilde{B}_{i}b,b^{\prime})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}=(b,\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}).

Also, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime\prime})\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)}+1<\beta_{i}+1,\\
&B_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime\prime})=\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}+1=\beta_{i},\\
&E_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime\prime})=(\varphi_{i}-1)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}+1=\beta_{i},\\
&\beta_{i}(b^{\prime\prime}_{1})=\beta_{i},\\
&\widetilde{E}_{i}b^{\prime\prime}_{2}=b_{2}\neq 0,\\
&\varphi_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{E}_{i}b^{\prime\prime}_{2})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}<\beta_{i}.\end{split}

This shows that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime\prime}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}b^{\prime\prime}_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{i}b^{\prime\prime}_{2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}b.\end{split}

The last identity shows that

(14)

\displaystyle(\widetilde{B}_{i}b,b^{\prime\prime})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}=(b,\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime\prime}).

Identities (
13
) and (
14
) confirm Definition
3.3.2
(
3
).

(4)

When
B_{i}(b)\leq E_{i}(b)=F_{i}(b)
and
\varphi_{i}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2})=\varphi_{i}
. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b)=E_{i}(b)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}=\varepsilon_{\tau(i)},\\
&\beta_{\tau(i)}(b)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b)-s_{i}=F_{i}(b)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}=\beta_{i}(b),\\
&\widetilde{B}_{i}b=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2}\notin\mathcal{B}.\end{split}

This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
4
), (
7b
), and (
7c
).

Let
b^{\prime}=b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes b^{\prime}_{2}\in\mathcal{B}
be such that
(\widetilde{B}_{i}b,b^{\prime})\neq 0
. Then, we have
b^{\prime}=b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2}
. By our assumption, we see that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F_{i}(b^{\prime})=\varphi_{i}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2})=\varphi_{i},\\
&B_{i}(b^{\prime})=B_{i}(b)\leq\varphi_{i},\\
&E_{i}(b^{\prime})=E_{i}(b)+1=\varphi_{i}+1,\end{split}

and hence,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b^{\prime})=E_{i}(b^{\prime})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{2})=\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{2})=\varepsilon_{\tau(i)}-1=\beta_{i}(b)-1,\\
&\beta_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b^{\prime})-s_{i}=(E_{i}(b^{\prime})-1)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{2})\neq\beta_{i}(b^{\prime}).\end{split}

This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
7d
).

Now, we compute as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}+1,\\
&B_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})=\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}+1\leq B_{i}(b)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}+1\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)}+1,\\
&E_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})=\varphi_{i}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}+1=\varphi_{i}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}+1=\varphi_{\tau(i)}+1,\\
&\beta_{i}(b^{\prime}_{1})=\beta_{i}\leq\varphi_{\tau(i)},\\
&\widetilde{F}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}_{2}=b_{2}\neq 0,\\
&\varphi_{i}(\widetilde{F}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}_{2})=\varphi_{i}=\varphi_{i}(b^{\prime}_{2}).\end{split}

Since
\varphi_{i}(\widetilde{F}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}_{2})=\varphi_{i}(b^{\prime}_{2})
is equivalent to
\varphi_{\tau(i)}(\widetilde{E}_{i}b^{\prime}_{2})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{2})-1
By Lemma
2.2.9
, this implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(b^{\prime}_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{i}b^{\prime}_{2}+b_{2}),\end{split}

which shows that

(\widetilde{B}_{i}b,b^{\prime})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}=(b,\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}).

This confirms Definition
3.3.2
(
3
).

(5)

When
F_{i}(b)\leq E_{i}(b)=B_{i}(b)=\beta_{\tau(i)}
and
\varphi_{i}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2})<E_{i}(b)
. Since

if
\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b_{1}\neq 0
. Also, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\beta_{i}(b)=E_{i}(b)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}=\varepsilon_{\tau(i)},\\
&\beta_{\tau(i)}(b)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}=B_{i}(b)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}=\beta_{i}(b),\\

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F_{i}(b^{\prime})=\varphi_{i}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2})<E_{i}(b),\\

&E_{i}(b^{\prime})=E_{i}(b)+1,\end{split}

and hence,

&\beta_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}(b^{\prime})-s_{i}=(E_{i}(b^{\prime})-1)+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}-\operatorname{wt}_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime}_{2})\neq\beta_{i}(b^{\prime}).\end{split}

3.3.2
(

Now, we compute as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&F_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})=\varphi_{\tau(i)}+1,\\
&B_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})=\beta_{\tau(i)}+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}+1=\varphi_{\tau(i)}+1,\\
&E_{\tau(i)}(b^{\prime})=\varphi_{i}(\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2})+\operatorname{wt}^{\imath}_{i}-s_{i}+1<\varphi_{\tau(i)}+1,\\
&\beta_{i}(b^{\prime}_{1})=\beta_{i}=\varphi_{\tau(i)}.\end{split}

This implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b^{\prime}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\widetilde{B}_{\tau(i)}b_{1}\otimes\widetilde{E}_{\tau(i)}b_{2}+b),\end{split}

which shows that

(
